    command line, so a sweep is a flag value and nothing else.

88.2 Semantics
    keep = 1 - m * natural_mortality        (NOT (1 - rate) * m)
    The factor multiplies the RATE. Consequences:
      m = 1   default, bit-identical to the pre-change engine.
      m = 0   exactly --mortality off.
      m > 1   harsher than the library; keep is clamped at 0 so biomass
              can never go negative.
      m < 0   rejected by the CLI and clamped by the environment; a
              negative rate would create biomass out of nothing.
    The flag only has an effect with --mortality on, because the branch
    in population_change is gated on apply_natural_mortality. It scales
    every FG by the same factor; per-FG scaling stays a library edit.

88.3 Where it lives
    The term is in the TICK, not in the reward path, so the flag applies
    to every reward mode (global, --local_reward, --legacy_reward,
    --integral_reward, --population-stability) and to inference.
      EcosystemEnvironment.mortality_multiplier   (clamped >= 0)
      population_change._apply_decision_maker_population_change (CPU)
      lib/gpu/ecosystem.py: mortality_keep        (GPU mirror)
    Plumbing: the flag itself is defined once, in
    population_change.add_mortality_multiplier_argument, and registered
    by train.py, lib/gpu/cli.py and inference.py - the same pattern as
    source_tracking.add_local_reward_arguments, so the parsers cannot
    drift apart. train.py carries it as the module global
    MORTALITY_MULTIPLIER (picklable through _EnvBuilder /
    _ProbeEnvBuilder into spawn workers, exactly like
    APPLY_NATURAL_MORTALITY); the GPU path carries it as a field on
    EnvironmentBuilder, including with_world. It is printed in both
    banners and written to biomass.jsonl's __meta__ header.

88.4 Tests
    tests/test_mortality_multiplier.py (21 cases): the default is the
    unscaled library rate, the factor scales the rate and not the
    survival fraction, m = 0 equals --mortality off, the flag is inert
    with mortality off, negative is clamped, a huge factor cannot drive
    biomass negative, the energy reserve follows the same factor, the
    GPU mortality_keep mirrors all of it, the CLI rejects negative/
    non-finite values, and with_world keeps the field.
    tests/test_gpu_ecosystem.py gained a per-tick CPU/GPU parity case
    for m in {0, 0.5, 2}. Suite: 459 passed, 12 skipped (was 432/12).

88.5 Checkpoints
    Unchanged. Observation layout, action layout and the FG set are
    untouched, so existing .pth files stay valid. Note the usual caveat:
    a policy trained at one m is evaluated under different mechanics at
    another m - loadable, not optimal.

89. BUGFIX: m = 0 WAS NOT --mortality off IN THE MONITORING ENVS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

89.1 The report
    "--mortality on --mortality_multiplier 0 gives very different
    extinction and growth behaviour than --mortality off, but they must
    be semantically identical." (Section 88.2 promises exactly that.)

89.2 What was actually equal, and what was not
    The TICK was already correct. A controlled 10-tick rollout on
    mareld2.yaml (20x20, same spawn/impact seeds) gives bit-identical
    per-FG biomass for (mortality=off) and (mortality=on, m=0), and
    differs from (mortality=on, m=1) as expected. The unit tests of
    88.4 cover the same branch.
    What was NOT equal were the environments built OUTSIDE the training
    rollout, i.e. the ones the user actually watches:
      a) lib/runners/training_progress.py - inference_config() listed
         project/library/grid/mortality/migration/currents but NOT
         mortality_multiplier, and build_inference_env() therefore
         constructed EcosystemEnvironment with the default 1.0. The
         survival evaluator (--eval-every, the progress tab) thus
         measured extinction and growth under the FULL library rates
         whenever --mortality on was given, no matter the factor.
      b) lib/gpu/visual.py - the live probe built _ProbeEnvBuilder
         positionally (project, grid, mortality, migration) and never
         passed the factor, so it fell back to train.py's module-level
         MORTALITY_MULTIPLIER, which in the GPU entry point is never
         assigned and stays 1.0. Same failure mode as (a).
    Both are exactly "different extinction and growth", and both only
    bite when mortality is on - which is why --mortality off looked fine.

89.3 The fix
    inference_config() now carries mortality_multiplier and
    build_inference_env() passes it on. Because the factor is part of
    progress/config.json, a --resume against a directory written before
    this change would fail the "evaluation settings changed" equality
    check; comparable_config() fills in the default 1.0 on both sides so
    old histories stay resumable (old runs did use the unscaled rates).
    lib/gpu/visual.py passes builder.mortality_multiplier explicitly.
    train.py now passes apply_natural_mortality / mortality_multiplier /
    migration explicitly to _ProbeEnvBuilder instead of relying on the
    module globals being set first - same values, no ordering trap.

89.4 Verification
    tests/test_mortality_multiplier.py section 6 (3 new cases): the
    progress config carries the factor, the progress env is built with
    it, and a config.json without the key still compares equal.
    Suite: 462 passed, 12 skipped. Plus 1-generation smoke runs of both
    flag combinations on 20x20.
    Note when reproducing by eye: the probe line varies a lot run to run
    because untrained FGs start from a fresh random policy - porpoises
    landed on 17/19/27/42 percent across four runs of BOTH settings. Do
    not read a single probe line as an A/B result.

90. THE VIABILITY RIG: "LIVSKRAFTIG" TURNED INTO A NUMBER (NO ARS)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------

90.1 Why
    Sections 73, 74 and 87.4 each end in the same place: the base has an
    effectively absorbing zero, the top predator cannot break even in a
    mean field, and the spawn sits away from the equilibrium. What was
    missing was not another observation but an ACCEPTANCE CRITERION: a
    definition of "long-term viable" that can be measured and signed off.
    --population-stability (POPULATION_STABILITY.md) is not that - it is
    reward shaping INSIDE training, so it mixes the world with the policy.
    Viability is a property of the parameters and the mechanisms in the
    tick, not of the policy nets, so it must be measurable with the
    behaviour FROZEN. That is what this arc adds.

90.2 The criterion (VIABILITY.md is authoritative)
    With B_f(t) the total biomass of FG f after tick t, T the horizon,
    W the last window_frac of the horizon, and
        eq_f   = mean over W of B_f(t)      (the MEASURED equilibrium)
        prev_f = mean over the window before W
        ref_f  = B_f(0)                    (the spawn level)
    a configuration is viable when, for EVERY FG present at spawn and
    EVERY seed:
        1 survival      B_f(t) > 0 for all t <= T
        2 floor         eq_f >= floor * ref_f            floor = 0.10
        3 ceiling       eq_f <= ceiling * ref_f          ceiling = 10.0
        4 stationarity  max(eq/prev, prev/eq) <= max_drift    = 2.0
    Horizon 5 years = 7300 ticks, 3 seeds (worst seed decides),
    window_frac 0.10. FGs absent at spawn are outside the criterion.
    Rationale: floor/ceiling are deliberately WIDE because the
    equilibrium is an output of the parameters, not an input - a group
    need not hold its spawn level, only avoid decaying to zero or running
    away. Criterion 4 is what makes the claim "long-term": it catches a
    group that is above the floor at T but still on a monotone slide.
    The criterion deliberately does NOT settle open vs closed system, nor
    does it gate on occupancy (with no environment maps in mareld2.yaml
    the grid is isotropic, so occ is not yet interpretable - see 87.5).
    occ is printed anyway, because surviving in one cell is qualitatively
    different from surviving across the grid.

90.3 The rig
    lib/diagnostics/viability.py  (criterion, arms, rollout, report)
    tools/viability.py            (CLI, exit code 0 = viable, 1 = not)
    Worlds come from inference.build_env(seed=...), i.e. fixed
    inference_initial_biomass with a seed-varying spawn layout, and the
    reference NumPy tick. The reward path is NEVER evaluated.
    Four frozen behaviour arms:
        neutral  no policy at all - PolicyController emits zero logits,
                 so the masked softmax is uniform over the legal actions
                 in each cell. Deterministic.
        eat      hand-coded: all mass on the legal prey in the cell,
                 never move, rest where there is nothing to eat. The
                 ecological upper bound on intake.
        random   freshly initialised PolicyNetwork per DM, seeded.
        policy   a checkpoint from results/<run-name>, for comparison.
    Defaults: --project mareld2.yaml --grid 20*20 --years 5 --seeds 3
    --behaviour neutral,eat --mortality off --migration off.
    Cost: ~18 s per neutral rollout and ~10 s per eat rollout of 7300
    ticks at 20x20, i.e. the default (2 arms x 3 seeds) runs in ~1.5
    minutes. This is the "runs in minutes, not hours" requirement.
    --json writes the machine-readable summary, --csv the trajectories.

90.4 First measurement: mareld2.yaml, 20x20, 5 years, mortality off
    Closed box (--migration off), worst of 3 seeds:

        arm      FG                 spawn  equilib  eq/spawn  drift  occ  extinct
        neutral  gadoids            277.8      0.0     0.000   1.00    0     3245
        neutral  pelagic_fish      1333.3      0.0     0.000   1.00    0     2192
        neutral  porpoises            4.4      0.0     0.000   1.00    0       27
        neutral  benthic_community 1777.8   3946.3     2.220   1.01  400        -
        neutral  phytoplankton     8888.9   6565.0     0.739   1.13  400        -
        neutral  zooplankton       3888.9  11816.6     3.039   5.16  400        - (drifting)
        eat      gadoids            277.8     20.4     0.073   1.14   43        - (below floor)
        eat      pelagic_fish      1333.3      0.0     0.000   1.00    0     4506
        eat      porpoises            4.4      0.0     0.000   1.00    0      396
        eat      benthic_community 1777.8   3273.3     1.841   1.02  400        -
        eat      phytoplankton     8888.9   9515.7     1.071   1.06  400        -
        eat      zooplankton       3888.9    496.8     0.128   1.15  399        -

    Verdict: NOT VIABLE in both arms. Three findings.
    a) The whole fish/predator column dies inside the horizon. Not just
       porpoises (which 87.5 already suspected): pelagic_fish goes at
       tick 2192 (1.5 years) and gadoids at 3245 (2.2 years) under
       neutral behaviour. Every reward comparison in sections 82-87 was
       measured over 1000-2500 ticks, i.e. BEFORE the collapse is
       visible. That is the single most important consequence.
    b) The arms disagree, so this is not a pure parameter statement.
       Under eat, gadoids survive the full 5 years (at 0.073x spawn) and
       porpoises last 396 ticks instead of 27. Behaviour buys time; it
       does not buy survival.
    c) zooplankton under neutral fails criterion 4, not the floor:
       drift 5.16 with min 0.009x spawn is a large slow oscillation, not
       an equilibrium. A 1000-tick rollout cannot see this at all.
    Note also 16x16/300 ticks, seed 20260530: porpoises survive the full
    300 ticks under eat but in ONE cell (occ = 1), and die at tick 26
    (neutral) / 148 (random). The mean-field energy budget of 74 is
    exactly what this is; the rig simply makes it a dated number.

90.5 Open vs closed changes almost nothing
    The same command with --migration on, worst of 3 seeds:
        neutral: gadoids extinct@2692, pelagic_fish extinct@2547,
                 porpoises extinct@23; zooplankton PASSES (drift 1.36,
                 eq/spawn 0.797) instead of failing on stationarity.
        eat:     the same verdicts as the closed box, and the levels
                 agree to three digits (gadoids 0.073x, pelagic_fish
                 extinct@4506, porpoises extinct@396) - unsurprising,
                 since the eat arm never moves, so almost nothing ever
                 reaches an edge.
    So the open-vs-closed question, which was left undecided, does NOT
    decide viability at this grid size: both settings are NOT VIABLE and
    the extinction ticks move by at most ~25 percent, in both directions.
    Caveat on interpretation: --migration on only re-interprets outflow
    through the grid edges as emigration plus an equal immigration. It is
    NOT recruitment from an external stock, so it is the weak form of
    "open system". A true open system (an immigration/recruitment term
    that does not require biomass to already be at the edge) is still
    untested, and is the version that would actually be expected to
    change the answer.

90.6 Tests
    tests/test_viability.py (31 cases): the horizon/window arithmetic,
    rejection of nonsensical criteria, each of the four conditions
    failing on its own (a flat trajectory passes; a settled level below
    the floor fails and still reports its equilibrium; runaway growth
    fails the ceiling; geometric growth fails stationarity while floor
    and ceiling hold; extinction reports its tick), FGs absent at spawn
    being outside the criterion, worst-seed aggregation, strict-JSON
    summary (no Infinity for an infinite drift), run_rollout booking
    spawn at t=0 and one sample per tick, the neutral arm installing no
    network and acting uniformly over the legal actions, the eat arm
    never moving and spending its mass only on legal prey, the random arm
    being reproducible per seed, unknown/unconfigured arms being refused,
    and the CLI (grid spellings n*m / nxm / n, unknown arms rejected,
    --ticks overriding --years, exit code carrying the verdict).
    Suite: 493 passed, 12 skipped (was 462/12). Plus a 1-generation
    train.py smoke run on 16x16.

90.7 What this does and does not settle
    Settled: "livskraftig" is now a number with a horizon, a seed count
    and four named conditions, measurable in ~1.5 minutes without ARS,
    and the current configuration fails it in a dated, reproducible way.
    The measured equilibrium per FG is now available, which is the input
    the spawn recalibration of 87.4 was missing.
    Not settled: the two structural holes of 73 and 74 (the absorbing
    base and the mean-field energy budget of the top predator), the true
    open-system variant (90.5), and whether a larger grid changes (a).
    Next step by this measurement: recalibrate spawn against equilib and
    re-run, then attack 74 - NOT more training. Nothing trained in this
    configuration can be interpreted past ~tick 2000.

91. THE VIABILITY RIG, SECOND PASS: TWO FACTORS AND A NORMATIVE CORNER
    (2026-09-21, after 90)

91.1 The two objections that forced the rebuild
    (a) "Why would a uniform or random policy failing to keep species
        alive for 5 years say anything about viability? Obviously it
        does not." Correct, and 90 was too blunt: it aggregated all arms
        into one viable true/false, so the trivially expected failure of
        neutral could set the whole verdict to NOT VIABLE.
    (b) "A hand-coded eat policy says nothing either, as long as the FGs
        are spawned beside each other rather than on top of each other."
        Also correct, and measurable. Overlap at spawn in mareld2.yaml,
        share of predator biomass in a cell that holds any prey, 60x60,
        three seeds:
            gadoids      -> pelagic_fish   0.44 / 0.24 / 0.31
            porpoises    -> pelagic_fish   1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00
            pelagic_fish -> zooplankton    1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00
            zooplankton  -> phytoplankton  1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00
        gadoids occupy ~1100/3600 cells and pelagic_fish ~1200/3600, so
        the product of the marginals is ~0.33: the measured overlap is
        exactly chance. Both are drawn as 25 independent colonies
        (colony, sigma 1.0) with no ecological link at tick 0. The eat
        arm sets move = 0, so that geometry is frozen forever.
    (c) Follow-up: "should rest not be represented in the greedy policy
        too?" Yes - rest has three distinct roles in the engine (cheap
        metabolism via cost_rest, hiding via prev_hidden_frac, and the
        mask fallback), and an arm without hiding is not an upper bound
        for an FG that is itself prey. But an arm that rests for energy
        can also "survive" by hibernating at low biomass, which would
        inflate a PASS. Hence hiding became its own arm, not a fifth
        action in greedy.

91.2 What the rig is now
    Two factors instead of one:
      behaviour: greedy | greedy_hide | eat | neutral | random | policy
      spawn:     colocated | configured
    The verdict is taken from the NORMATIVE CORNER = colocated + greedy.
    Only greedy, greedy_hide and eat may decide at all; a run of
    neutral/random alone reports NO VERDICT and exits 0 (objection (a)).
    The differences between cells are the information:
      greedy - eat          = what search is worth
      greedy_hide - greedy  = what the predation refuge is worth
      colocated - configured = how much is spawn geometry

91.3 The greedy arm, and two ways of getting it wrong
    Rule per cell: move to the neighbour with the highest reachable
    intake if that beats staying, else eat over the legal prey present,
    else rest (mask fallback only - no voluntary rest).
    (i) The field must be the PAYOFF, not raw prey biomass: energy
        intake per unit predator biomass, i.e. holling_a_eff (Holling
        II/III + Beddington-DeAngelis interference) times energy_gain,
        summed over the menu - exactly predation.apply_predation's
        demand with B_pred, hunger and the eat share divided out. With
        raw biomass the gradient has no saturation, every FG piles into
        the single richest cell (pelagic_fish occ 1 after 20 ticks on
        8x8) and the arm manufactures the shoal density 74 says a 1 km
        mean field cannot sustain - i.e. it would answer a question
        about its own artefact.
    (ii) The potential must be a MAX, not a sum, and travel must pay for
        itself. P(c) = max(payoff(c), 0.85 * max P(neighbour)) (value
        iteration; converges to max_d payoff(d) * 0.85^dist), and a cell
        moves only when 0.85 * P(best neighbour) > payoff(c). A diffusive
        (summed) potential peaks at the prey's centre of mass rather than
        at the best cell, so every FG walks to the middle and starves:
        8x8/40 ticks, porpoises 0.03x spawn under the summed version and
        0.00 (extinct@28) when comparing potentials without the travel
        discount, against 0.51x for simply standing still under eat. An
        arm that is worse than eat is not an upper bound. With both fixes
        greedy >= eat for every FG in that test.
    0.85 per km is the only free parameter, and it has one meaning: what
    a tick of travel costs relative to a tick of feeding.

91.4 greedy_hide
    Same as greedy, but rests when the predator biomass in the cell is
    at least 1.0x the group's own biomass there (HIDE_PREDATOR_RATIO),
    or when the energy reserve is at least 95 % full (HIDE_SATIATION) so
    intake buys nothing. Both are named module constants.

91.5 Co-located spawn
    colocate_spawn() moves every predator onto the richest cells of its
    own prey, bottom up through the food web (topological sort on the
    menus, so pelagic_fish is placed before gadoids/porpoises are placed
    on it). It keeps the group's TOTAL BIOMASS and its NUMBER OF
    OCCUPIED CELLS, so per-cell density and patchiness are unchanged and
    only the geometry moves. It is deliberately generous: it answers
    "can this parameterisation carry an ecosystem given a perfect start",
    not "is the layout in fg_library.yaml viable".

91.6 Result, 20x20, 5 years (7300 ticks), 3 seeds, worst seed, closed box
    Extinction tick (or eq/spawn when alive at the end):
                     colocated              configured
      greedy         gadoids   ext@4943     gadoids   ext@4823
                     pel_fish  0.000x       pel_fish  0.000x
                     porpoises ext@176      porpoises ext@145
      greedy_hide    gadoids   ext@4564     gadoids   0.000x (occ 9)
                     pel_fish  ext@2906     pel_fish  ext@1776
                     porpoises ext@176      porpoises ext@91
      eat            gadoids   ext@2938     gadoids   0.073x
                     pel_fish  ext@3440     pel_fish  ext@4506
                     porpoises ext@396      porpoises ext@396
    Overlap at tick 0, 20x20, seed 20260530 (share of predator biomass
    in a cell with prey / prey density seen relative to the grid mean):
      configured: gadoids->pelagic_fish 0.168 / 0.21
                  porpoises->pelagic_fish 1.000 / 22.42
      colocated:  gadoids->pelagic_fish 0.942 / 2.86
                  porpoises->pelagic_fish 1.000 / 4.34
    Verdict: NOT VIABLE in the normative corner.

91.7 What the second pass changed in the conclusions of 90
    (a) The gadoids FAIL is NOT mainly a spawn artefact. That was the
        working hypothesis after the overlap measurement in 91.1(b), and
        the rig refutes it: with search enabled, gadoids die at 4943
        (colocated) vs 4823 (configured) - a 2.5 percent difference,
        even though the overlap goes from 0.168 to 0.942. Geometry is
        worth almost nothing once the group is allowed to move. What
        geometry did explain is the DIFFERENCE BETWEEN ARMS: under the
        stationary eat arm, colocating gadoids makes things WORSE
        (ext@2938 vs 0.073x alive), because the cod are placed on the
        herring, eat them out, and then cannot leave.
    (b) Search buys time, not viability: greedy extends gadoids from
        2938 to 4943 ticks (+68 percent) over eat, and keeps
        pelagic_fish nominally alive instead of extinct at 3440 - but
        both still fail floor.
    (c) porpoises die at 91-396 ticks in EVERY cell of the table, under
        the most favourable start (100 percent overlap, 22x the grid
        mean prey density under the configured spawn) and the strongest
        frozen behaviour. This is 74's mean-field energy budget, full
        stop: it is not behaviour, not training and not geometry.
    (d) greedy_hide is NOT uniformly better than greedy: hiding costs
        feeding ticks, and pelagic_fish dies earlier with it (2906 vs
        alive). Hiding is a real trade-off in this engine, not a free
        survival bonus - which is also why it must not outrank greedy
        for the verdict.

91.8 Tests
    tests/test_viability.py grew from 31 to 44 cases: only a normative
    arm may decide (neutral/random alone -> viable is None), greedy
    outranks eat when both ran, the greedy action tensor is a
    distribution over legal actions only with no voluntary rest, the
    move set equals the travel-discount rule exactly, a 15 percent
    improvement does not buy a lost feeding tick while a 100 percent one
    does, the potential peaks at the best cell (not the centre of mass)
    and decays by exactly 0.85 per cell, greedy_hide rests on a full
    reserve and under predation pressure, trophic_order puts prey first
    and survives a cycle, co-location conserves total biomass and cell
    count and cannot lower any overlap, and the overlap report carries
    both numbers. Suite: 506 passed, 12 skipped (was 493/12).

91.9 Next by this measurement
    Unchanged from 90.7 in direction, sharper in content: the top
    predator (74) is now proven to be a structural problem under the
    most generous conditions the rig can construct, so it is the first
    thing to fix - sub-grid patchiness/aggregation term, conspecific
    attraction in movement, or a redefinition of the porpoise energy
    budget. Spawn recalibration against equilib (87.4) is still needed
    but will not save gadoids or pelagic_fish on its own, since both
    also fail with search and perfect co-location. What is NOT worth
    doing yet: more training, more reward shaping, and impacts.

92. THE MEASURE BEFORE THE ECOLOGY: MIXED-DIET BREAK-EVEN, THE RIG'S
    DIET, AND THE PORPOISE INTAKE CEILING

92.0 Why this arc exists
    User principle, and it turned out to be the right one: the
    nutritional values in fg_library.yaml are calibrated against the
    literature, so if the diagnostics disagree with the library the
    diagnostics are the first suspect, not the biology. Recomputing
    the porpoise budget in the units the literature uses confirmed
    the library and falsified two of this agent's own measures. The
    three items below are corrections of measurement error that were
    introduced by the agent, and they were done BEFORE any further
    ecological interpretation.

    Porpoise budget in literature units, straight off the library:

      resting metabolism   360 MJ/t/day -> 18 MJ/day for a 50 kg animal
      feeding              504 MJ/t/day -> 25 MJ/day (~4x Kleiber)
      reserve 2800 MJ/t    = 5.6 days of starvation
      realised ration      8.9 % of body mass/day (Kastelein 4-9.5 %)

    Metabolism, reserve and ration are all in range. The requirement
    on diet quality then follows arithmetically:

      504 / 0.089 = 5670 MJ/t assimilated = 6.3 MJ/kg gross
        herring  7.5 MJ/kg * 0.90 = 6750 MJ/t   clears by 19 %
        gadoid   5.5 MJ/kg * 0.84 = 4620 MJ/t   does not pay alone

92.1 Item 1a: the break-even measure is over the RATION, not the pair
    predation.apply_predation sums actual_intake over the whole prey
    axis, multiplies by energy_gain_mat and hands the predator ONE
    number; get_hunger and the feeding cost are applied once, to that
    total. A per-pair break-even is therefore not a quantity the tick
    ever evaluates. The correct statement for a mixed menu is the
    minimum share of the high-quality prey in the ration:

      f * 6750 + (1 - f) * 4620 >= 5670   ->   f >= 0.493

    At least 49.3 % clupeid in the ration. Kattegat / Skagerrak
    stomach data give 50-70 % clupeids by mass, so the library is
    inside the window, with little room to spare.

92.2 74.2b is WITHDRAWN
    Section 74.2b read sat_min = 1.227 for porpoises -> gadoids as
    ARITHMETICALLY INFEASIBLE and recommended fixing or removing the
    pair. That was a misreading of the agent's own diagnostic. Gadoid
    is not pure loss, it is low-quality food, and a porpoise that has
    to live on it alone would need ~12 % of its body mass per day,
    i.e. at or above the physiological cap. That is the junk-food
    hypothesis (MacLeod et al. 2007; Spitz et al. 2012) emerging
    correctly from the parameters. "Fixing" the pair would have meant
    writing against the literature. The pair stays; 74.8 item 4 is
    withdrawn and 74.2b carries a SUPERSEDED marker.

92.3 Item 1b: tools/probes/budget_gate.py runs again
    It had been unrunnable since the action costs moved into the env
    caches: it read pred.feeding_cost, which no longer exists on
    FunctionalGroup (AttributeError), so every number section 74
    rests on was irreproducible. Now reads dm_resting_metabolism /
    dm_cost_eat and prints two blocks:

      * per (predator, prey): the old table, with the verdict column
        renamed "alone" and INFEASIBLE renamed "not alone", because
        that is all a per-pair failure means,
      * per predator: ration (t/t/tick and % body mass/day), cost,
        need_q, best and worst prey quality, and "min share" - the
        minimum share of the best prey in the ration.

    Live mareld2, 16x16:

      predator      ration  %bm/day  cost  need_q  best  worst  share
      gadoids      0.01500     6.0%  10.5     700  6000   2100  any
      pelagic_fish 0.01500     6.0%  15.2    1013  3600   3600  any
      porpoises    0.02223     8.9% 126.0    5669  6750   4620  0.492
      zooplankton  0.09375    37.5%   5.1      54  1300   1300  any

    Only the porpoise needs a mixed diet at all; every other DM
    clears the bar on any prey on its menu.

92.4 Item 2: the rig was feeding the top predator junk food
    Both hand-coded arms in lib/diagnostics/viability.py allocated
    the eat mass EVENLY over the legal prey present (eat = mask /
    total). For a porpoise in a cell with both prey that is a 50/50
    diet:

      even split  5685 MJ/t  against a requirement of 5670 MJ/t
                  -> a margin of 3 parts per thousand
      herring     6750 MJ/t  -> a margin of 19 %

    So the "normative" arm put the top predator three per mille from
    starvation and then reported its death as a property of the
    world. Same class of error as the spawn geometry of section 91.

    The fix follows the engine. Intake is LINEAR in the per-prey eat
    share up to the per-pair harvest cap MAX_HARVEST_FRAC * B_prey,
    so the energy-maximising diet is water-filling in descending
    order of marginal yield energy_gain_j * a_eff_j:

      cap_j  = MAX_HARVEST_FRAC * B_j / (B_pred * a_eff_j * h)
      shares = fill the best prey to cap_j, then the next, ...

    New helpers: _per_prey_intake (the a_eff tensor, formerly folded
    into _intake_payoff), _saturating_share (the caps), _waterfill
    (the allocation) and _eat_shares (mask handling + the leftover
    rule). Leftover share, once every legal prey is saturated, goes
    to the best of them: it buys no energy but the population still
    pays the feeding cost, which keeps the arms comparable with the
    even-split runs.

    _intake_payoff changed meaning as well, and had to. It used to
    SUM the per-prey rates with an implicit share of 1.0 on each,
    which is not a quantity the engine can deliver since the shares
    are a distribution. It now weights by the chosen diet and clips
    each term at the harvest cap, so the greedy potential no longer
    walks towards a cell whose prey the group has already eaten.

    Note for anyone extending this: the criterion is marginal YIELD,
    not energy content. A cell dense in gadoid can out-yield the
    same cell's thin herring, and eating the gadoid there is the
    right answer - the first version of the test asserted a herring
    preference per cell and was correctly falsified by the rig.

92.5 Item 3: max_intake_rate is a ceiling, not a ration
    porpoises.max_intake_rate was 0.05 = 20 % of body mass per day,
    2x the highest ration ever measured, while the realised ration
    came out right only because satiation_scale 0.9 - documented in
    lib/world/energy_balance.py as "a pure model constant (no
    literature anchor)" - cut it back to 8.9 %/day. The
    literature-anchored parameter was wrong and the un-anchored one
    was doing its job. Applied:

      max_intake_rate      0.05 -> 0.035   (14 %bm/day, ~1.5x the
                                            highest observed ration)
      handling_time        20.0 -> 28.6    (both porpoise pairs;
                                            1/h = a is the library
                                            convention, so a*h = 1)
      satiation_scale       0.9 -> 1.37    (keeps a*h(u_X) = 0.0222
                                            t/t/tick = 8.9 %bm/day)

    Invariant by construction: a*h(u_X), and therefore sat_min
    (0.840), the break-even visible density (5.25 t/cell) and the
    GUI gate margin (net_eat = +24.0 MJ/t/tick). Measured effect on
    the rig: porpoise extinction tick 1369 -> 1396 (+2 %), i.e.
    hygiene, as intended.

    Two things are NOT invariant and were chosen deliberately:

      * satiation_scale now exceeds 1.0. At 0.9 a porpoise with a
        full reserve stopped feeding altogether, which treats a
        5.6-day blubber store as if it were stomach content. A
        mammal at full condition still eats. The gate now flattens
        the intake curve instead of closing it.
      * The lower bound on max_intake_rate is not free. The legacy
        full-hunger gate G4 in evaluate_energy_balance requires
        ceiling > rest_cost + feed_cost = 216 MJ/t/tick, i.e.
        a > 0.032. The literature-preferred 0.03 (12 %bm/day) fails
        it, which is why 0.035 was chosen. Worth knowing: the
        porpoise clears G4 by 22 %, and G4 is the one gate in that
        module that is explicitly legacy.

92.6 Measurements, normative corner, 20x20, 5 years, 3 seeds, worst
     seed, closed box (--spawn colocated --behaviour greedy)

      state                       porpoises  gadoids       pelagic_fish
      before (91, even split)          176   ext@5030      below floor
      after item 2 (diet)             1369   below floor   below floor
      after item 3 (parameters)       1396   below floor   below floor
      full 3-arm rerun                1404   ext@4741      below floor

    The diet correction is worth a factor 7.8 on the porpoise and
    takes gadoids off the extinction list in two of three runs; the
    parameter change is worth 2 %. Run-to-run jitter in the
    extinction tick is under 1 % (1396 vs 1404 for identical seeds),
    which is float32 reduction order, so differences below ~50 ticks
    should not be read as signal.

    All three arms after the changes (extinction tick, worst seed):

      arm           porpoises  pelagic_fish  gadoids
      eat                 542          3494     3017
      greedy             1404             -     4741
      greedy_hide        1202          2400     4246

    greedy > greedy_hide > eat for the porpoise, which is the
    expected ordering and the first time the arms have ranked
    sensibly for it: search is worth 2.6x standing still, and hiding
    costs a top predator feeding ticks for nothing.

    Verdict UNCHANGED: NOT VIABLE in the normative corner. What
    changed is that it is now a statement about the world rather
    than about the arm. The three FAILs that remain are also more
    clearly separated: the porpoise dies at 1404 with a perfect
    start, an optimal search and the best available diet, which is
    still section 74's resolution problem; pelagic_fish settles at
    0.0008x spawn with drift 1.82, i.e. it has not even finished
    falling; gadoids follows its prey down.

92.7 Tests
    tests/test_viability.py 44 -> 49: water-filling takes the best
    prey first and spills into the next, all-in when no cap binds,
    the whole action mass is always spent, the shares are a
    distribution over legal prey only, the chosen diet is never
    worse than the even split on the energy the engine would
    actually deliver (and strictly better somewhere), and the top
    predator obeys water-filling optimality per cell and never
    produces a 50/50 split everywhere.

    tests/test_energy_balance_gate.py +3: no DM is infeasible on its
    whole menu (the correct replacement for the per-pair
    INFEASIBLE), the porpoise needs a clupeid majority but stays
    inside the 50-70 % window the stomach data supply, and the
    porpoise intake ceiling is 10-16 %bm/day while the realised
    ration is inside the Kastelein 4-9.5 % window and strictly below
    the ceiling.

    Suite: 514 passed, 12 skipped (was 506/12).

92.8 What this does and does not change
    Does not change: the verdict, the direction of 91.9, the
    blocking of retraining, or the status of impacts.
    Does change: every porpoise number in sections 74, 90 and 91 was
    measured with an even-split diet and, from 91 onwards, is a
    lower bound on what the arm can do rather than a statement about
    the parameters. 74.2b is withdrawn outright. The porpoise
    intake parameters in fg_library.yaml are new, so any .pth
    checkpoint trained before this section was trained against a
    different intake ceiling - the observation layout is untouched,
    so the checkpoints still LOAD, but they are no longer calibrated.

92.9 Next by this measurement
    1. pelagic_fish is now the least understood of the three: drift
       1.82 at 0.0008x spawn means the horizon does not even contain
       its equilibrium. It needs the same causal treatment section
       74 gave the porpoise - break-even identity plus a loss budget
       under the normative corner - before anything is changed.
    2. The remaining items of the revised order: decide the herring
       energy content deliberately (annual mean 7.5 MJ/kg vs a
       seasonal 9.5, which alone takes need_vis from 5.25 to 1.98
       t/cell against a field mean of 3.33 - this is a literature
       CHOICE and needs the user), then the zooplankton headroom
       (1.20) and seabirds (1.50), then school cohesion.
    3. maintenance_level still carries two incompatible roles (body
       condition setpoint and satiation level). As long as one
       number does both, neither can be calibrated honestly. This is
       the same conflation satiation_scale 1.37 works around for the
       porpoise only.

93 SCROLLING CLOUD CURRENTS (2026-09-22)

93.1 Implementation
    Replaced the spatially uniform random current vector with smooth,
    seeded value noise scrolling in +X/+Y (east/south in array coordinates).
    movement.py owns noise generation, direction fractions and CPU passive
    transport. currents.py retains CurrentConfig, CLI and compatibility
    exports. TensorEcosystem.advect samples the same field on the device.

    The cloud mixes two spatial scales with weights 0.75 and 0.25.
    Coordinates are ((x - tick/period)/scale, (y - tick/period)/scale).
    The outgoing biomass fraction is strength * cloud * current_response,
    split equally east/south, with identical transport of energy reserves.
    Blocked habitat/edge transfers stay at the source, even with migration.
    No active movement cost, ecology RNG consumption, or evolving texture
    state. The existing step hook still runs before growth and independently
    of decision-maker movement. Coordinates are cached per environment.

93.2 Configuration and compatibility
    --currents remains off by default. --current-strength still caps the
    outgoing fraction per tick (default 0.1). --current-period now means
    ticks to scroll one cell along each axis (default 20), NOT ticks between
    random vector targets. New --current-scale is the coarse noise lattice
    spacing in cells (default 12, minimum 1). Scrolling speed and transport
    strength are independent. Metadata/probe propagation retains scale.

    Per-FG current_response is validated in [0,1], default 1 for existing
    species definitions. The library sets phytoplankton=1 and
    benthic_community=0. All decision makers, including zooplankton, remain
    excluded. Susceptibility is editable in the library YAML.

    This is diagonal drift with spatially varying strength, not an eddy or
    hydrodynamic model. Persistent flow can accumulate biomass downstream.
    Old current-enabled trajectories change, but the observation/checkpoint
    layout does not. CURRENTS.md documents parameters and migration semantics.

93.3 Validation
    Environment: .venv/bin/python, Torch 2.11.0, NumPy 1.26.4, no CUDA.
    Current tests: 32 passed, 9 CUDA cases skipped. Coverage includes spatial
    NumPy/Torch parity, continuous positive-XY scrolling, seeds, conservation,
    habitat/edges, response 0/0.25/1, no decision makers, CLI/metadata, ARS
    chunk independence, fullgraph tracing and no tensor readback.

    Full suite: 450 passed, 72 skipped, 2 failed. An isolated git archive of
    unchanged HEAD reproduced BOTH failures (432 passed, 68 skipped):
      * test_live_project_porpoises_need_a_clupeid_majority_not_a_pure_diet:
        computed minimum pelagic share 0.266912, below expected 0.40.
      * test_full_ticks_match_reference[cpu-0.0-True-True]: currents disabled;
        reserve difference 0.00027227 exceeds existing parity tolerance.
    Neither unrelated failure was modified or hidden by changing tolerances.

    One CPU training generation passed at 60x60, pelagic_fish, 2 deltas,
    3 worlds, 30 ticks, currents on (strength 0.2, period 10, scale 9.5):
      results/current-cloud-smoke-20260922/
    One tensor-trainer generation passed on CPU/eager at 8x9, 2 deltas,
    2 worlds, 15 ticks, all active DMs and the same current settings:
      results/current-cloud-tensor-smoke-20260922/
    Syntax and diff checks passed. CUDA execution still needs NVIDIA hardware.

94 FIXED BIOMASS HEATMAP COLOURS (2026-09-22)

    User explicitly requested fixed colour maxima based on starting biomass.
    This supersedes section 75.2(a) for heatmaps. Each FG now uses its own
    rollout-start per-cell maximum, multiplied by the optional display-scale
    slider (default 1x). Declining biomass darkens; values above the reference
    saturate. No frame-dependent maximum fallback, including zero starts.
    New rollouts capture a new reference; replay and log mode retain it.
    Clicking the slider label restores 1x rather than automatic heatmap
    scaling. The line plot's automatic axis remains the default.

    inference.run_inference now records tick 0 BEFORE the first simulation
    step and records subsequent biomass frames as ticks 1..N. Previously its
    tick-0 frame already included one ecological update. Training probes
    already supplied the true initial field.

    Verified actual rendered pixel colours with SDL dummy across decline,
    growth, log mode, slider/reset, new rollout, replay and a zero start.
    Three-tick real-policy inference retained the original maxima and
    recorded four frames (ticks 0..3). One-generation visual tensor training
    passed on CPU/eager with currents on; outputs:
      results/fixed-biomass-colours-smoke-20260922/

95 CURRENT BOUNDARIES FOLLOW MIGRATION (2026-09-22)

    User observed current-driven biomass collecting at edges and requested
    the same migration handling as ordinary movement. Reproduced with 100
    units at the southeast corner: --migration on still left all 100 there.
    CPU currents forced a closed movement mask; tensor currents multiplied
    their mask by neighbor_valid, also blocking every off-grid outflow.

    Current transport now respects env.migration on both backends. With
    migration on, edge outflows are summed and reintroduced with the same
    habitat-weighted immigration and threshold concentration as swimmers.
    The existing immigration helpers now accept the participating FG IDs
    or tensor indices, defaulting to DMs for existing movement callers.
    Biomass and reserve remain conserved; inaccessible internal cells stay
    blocked. With migration off the existing closed boundaries remain.
    This is boundary redistribution, not opposite-edge wrapping, and does
    not guarantee a uniform spatial equilibrium.

    Add --migration on to inference as well as --currents on; neither flag
    changes its default. CURRENTS.md now documents both boundary modes.
    This supersedes the always-closed-current description in section 93.

    Regression: corner release with migration on/off, habitat-weighted
    recipient ratios, no immigration to blocked cells, CPU/tensor agreement
    with unequal DM/NDM counts, and NDM-only worlds including 1x1/1xN/Nx1.
    ARS chunking/fullgraph/no-readback checks also run with migration on;
    CUDA capture cases now enable migration (skipped here: no NVIDIA GPU).
    Full suite: 457 passed, 74 skipped, same 2 pre-existing failures as 93.3.
    Syntax and diff checks passed. One generation completed in both trainers
    with currents and migration on, strength 0.2, period 10, scale 9.5:
      results/current-migration-smoke-20260922/
      results/current-migration-tensor-smoke-20260922/

96 --rnd_baseline [all|solo|none] ON train_gpu.py AND inference.py (2026-09-22)

96.1 Why
    The random-action baseline (section 62) existed with all three
    modes on train.py only. train_gpu.py had none, and inference.py had
    a store_true flag equivalent to 'all'. The leave-one-out 'solo'
    mode is the fair per-DM ablation ("how much is FG k's learned
    behaviour worth against the trained rest"), so it is now on all
    three entry points with the section 62 semantics: bare flag = all,
    absent = none, solo = one world per DM with only that DM random.

96.2 Shared helper
    lib/runners/rnd_baseline.py: add_rnd_baseline_argument (the CLI,
    same choices/const/default as train.py) and rnd_baseline_plot_ids
    ('all' -> every FG incl. NDMs, 'solo' -> DMs only, 62.5). train.py
    itself is unchanged.

96.3 train_gpu.py
    The GPU viewer already runs train._probe_biomass on CPU between
    updates, so the baseline is that probe's own rnd path: lib/gpu/
    visual.py passes rnd_builder=<probe builder> and rnd_mode, and the
    viewer registers the _rnd legend ids. --rnd_baseline without
    --visual is a CLI error (there is no probe headless).
    policy_snapshot now also carries legacy_reward, integral_reward,
    alpha, beta, survival_bonus, survival_threshold,
    population_stability and local_reward (LocalRewardConfig) from
    trainer.runner_options. Before this, the _rnd reward curve would
    silently have used the probe's getattr defaults instead of the
    run's reward formula. Only the rnd path of the probe reads these
    fields, so the main probe is unchanged. The GPU update is never
    touched.

96.4 inference.py
    run_inference(..., rnd_env=None, rnd_solo_envs=None): rnd_env is
    the old 'all' world; rnd_solo_envs = {dm_id: env}. Internally the
    same rnd_env_for_fid / unique_rnd_envs structure as the train.py
    probe. _install_rnd_policies gives the random DMs RandomPolicy and
    mean=0/var=1, and the other DMs the trained policy and the main
    env's frozen obs-norm rows. Per-DM forward path (_batched_ready =
    False), as before. _push_action_fracs got an 'only' filter and a
    per-env prev snapshot, so each solo world reports only its random
    DM. The early stop needs the main world AND every baseline world
    dead.
    Two CLI behaviour changes:
      * --rnd_baseline without --visual now warns and is ignored (it
        only feeds the live plot; headless 'solo' would cost N
        rollouts for nothing).
      * With --rnd_baseline and no --seed a seed is drawn and printed.
        Every world is built separately, and an unseeded spawn gave
        the old 'all' baseline a different layout from the main world.
        Drawing the seed keeps the main world exactly as random as an
        unseeded run.

96.5 Caveat (unchanged from 62, now documented)
    The tick shuffles the FG order with the GLOBAL numpy RNG, and the
    env constructor draws season phases from it. Worlds stepped side by
    side therefore do not see the same tick order, so a solo curve
    differs from the main curve by FG k's randomness PLUS order noise.
    Season phases do not matter while seasonal_amplitude is 0 in the
    library. The same holds for the train.py probe.

96.6 Tests
    tests/test_rnd_baseline.py: CLI semantics match train.py; plot ids
    per mode; solo randomises exactly one DM per world, keeps the
    trained policies identical objects, and reports only DMs; a solo
    world whose random DM is swapped back to its policy reproduces the
    main world (global RNG pinned), so any divergence is caused by the
    one random DM; 'all' keeps its old behaviour; GPU viewer draws
    DM _rnd curves in both modes and phytoplankton_rnd only in 'all';
    GPU CLI refuses --rnd_baseline without --visual;
    policy_snapshot carries the reward settings.
    NOT RUN by the agent that wrote them: that environment could not
    install torch. Run the full suite plus a 1-generation training.

97 CONFIGURABLE TICK LENGTH (2026-09-22)

97.1 Why
    The tick was 6 hours by assumption, not by configuration. The
    number appeared as a literal in labels ("fraction/tick" without a
    duration), in tick <-> real-time conversions (TICKS_PER_YEAR = 1460
    in lib/diagnostics/viability.py, TICKS_PER_DAY = 4.0 in
    tools/starve_calibration.py and tools/probes/budget_gate.py), in
    plot axes and in every grid_config dict. Changing it meant editing
    all of them and hoping none was missed.

97.2 The engine was already tick-agnostic
    Worth stating because it shaped the design. The tick pipeline never
    asks how long a tick is: every library parameter is expressed PER
    TICK and Grid.tick_duration is stored but read nowhere. The tick
    length is therefore purely INTERPRETIVE - it converts a per-tick
    rate into a real-world rate and lets a label name a duration. No
    change to the tick was needed or made.

97.3 Where the value lives
    project_metadata.tick_hours, next to reference_grid_{width,height}.
    Whole hours, 1 to 168, default 6. Missing / out of range resolves
    to 6, so a project file predating the field loads bit-identically.

    lib/world/tick_time.py is the single definition point, same pattern
    as HUNGER_SATIATION_SCALE in energy_balance.py: DEFAULT/MIN/MAX,
    resolve_tick_hours, ticks_per_day, ticks_per_year, tick_label,
    per_tick, and the rescale rules. Read it through
    config_loader.project_tick_hours() / library_tick_hours(); do not
    hardcode 4 ticks/day again.

97.4 The GUI
    Project Info gained a third row: "Tick Length (hours, 1-168)" with
    an Entry, an Apply button and a live-validation status label,
    mirroring the Reference Grid row. Typing is validated on keypress;
    a valid but un-applied value shows "ej tillampad -> Apply" in
    amber rather than red.

    Every FG-editor label whose unit is per tick now carries the
    duration: "Max Growth (MG_X, fraction/6 h)", "Max Intake Rate (ton
    prey / ton consumer / 6 h)", "Seasonal Period (ticks of 6 h)".
    Labels are templates containing "{tick}"; _make_prop_label
    registers them and _refresh_tick_labels re-renders on change.
    Resting Metabolism gained the "/tick" it was always missing - it
    is MJ/ton/tick in energy_balance.py and the label said MJ/ton.

97.5 Rescaling: what Apply does
    Apply rescales the LIBRARY so the biology stays constant in real
    time, then stamps library_metadata.tick_hours. Per dimension:

      flux    x k        linear; the quantity genuinely accumulates
                         within a tick. max_intake_rate,
                         resting_metabolism, seed_rate.
      growth  (1+r)^k-1  a per-tick fraction ADDED to a stock.
                         growth_rate.
      loss    1-(1-r)^k  a per-tick fraction REMOVED from a stock, i.e.
                         a survival probability. natural_mortality,
                         starve_rate, movement_speed.
      period  p / k      a duration in ticks, floored at 1 whole tick.
                         seasonal_period.

    Compounding rather than linear for the fraction classes is not
    cosmetic: linear mortality at k = 28 exceeds 1, and a linear
    growth_rate can drive `1 + rate * surplus` negative in
    population_change._apply_decision_maker_population_change.

    Everything else is tick-independent and is left alone: stocks
    (max_energy_reserve, energy_content, max_carrying_capacity,
    initial_biomass_*), dimensionless thresholds (maintenance_level,
    satiation_scale, visibility_floor, extinction_threshold_factor,
    seasonal_amplitude), the action-cost multipliers (feeding_cost,
    resting_cost, movement_cost - they multiply resting_metabolism,
    which is itself rescaled) and interference / min_split_biomass.

    A converted value that exceeds its declared range is CLAMPED and
    listed as such in the confirmation dialog - never silently cut.
    Zooplankton max_intake_rate 0.25 at 6 -> 168 h is the live example:
    7.0 clamped to 1.0, biology not preserved, and the dialog says so.

97.6 The shared-library problem, and the stamp
    fg_library.yaml is shared between projects and is where the runtime
    reads every biological parameter from (load_project_config merges
    only spawn / initial_biomass_* / muted off the project entry).
    Rescaling it for one project therefore changes every other project
    that loads it.

    That cannot be designed away, so it is made visible instead:
    library_metadata.tick_hours records the tick length the library's
    numbers are calibrated at, the confirmation dialog says the library
    is shared, and opening a project whose tick_hours disagrees with
    the library raises a mismatch warning naming the factor. An
    unstamped library reads as 6 h, which is what it is.

    save_project persists the COMMITTED tick length, never the raw
    Entry: an un-applied edit has not rescaled anything, so writing it
    would record a tick length the numbers do not match.

97.7 Consumers updated
    lib/diagnostics/viability.py   ViabilityCriterion.tick_hours; ticks
                                   = years * ticks_per_year(tick_hours).
                                   TICKS_PER_YEAR kept as the 6 h value
                                   because tools/viability.py converts a
                                   --ticks override back into years.
    tools/viability.py             reads project_tick_hours(--project),
                                   prints the tick line.
    tools/probes/budget_gate.py    %bm/day and the units footer.
    tools/starve_calibration.py    TICKS_PER_DAY resolved per run; the
                                   LITERATURE targets are in days.
    run_mareld_mvp.py              plot axis + grid_config.
    lib/world/grid.py              tick_duration defaults to the shared
                                   constant instead of a bare 6.0.

    NOT threaded through: the 'tick_duration': 6.0 literals in
    train.py, inference.py, manual_play.py, simple_inference.py,
    compare_scenarios.py and the ab_test_* scripts. Grid.tick_duration
    is read nowhere, so these are inert; converting them is a separate
    tidy-up, not a correctness fix.

97.8 Tests
    tests/test_tick_time.py (78 cases): defaults and the 1-168 bounds;
    legacy projects resolving to 6; label rendering incl. whole days
    ("7 d" for 168 h); the identity, the A->B->A round trip per rule,
    and that unknown keys are never touched; per-class dimensional
    behaviour; clamping reported not silent; zeros staying zero
    (phytoplankton movement_speed); and the two real-time invariance
    properties that are the point of the feature - survival over a
    fixed real duration and intake over a fixed real duration are
    unchanged by the tick length. Plus the loader accessors and that
    mareld2.yaml and fg_library.yaml agree.

    The period round trip is exact only to within one tick of the
    coarser of the two lengths: 1460 h is not a whole number of 168 h
    steps. That is quantisation, not a bug, and the test states the
    bound rather than hiding it.

    Suite: 625 passed, 15 skipped, 7 failed - the SAME 7 that fail on
    a clean worktree at HEAD (verified with a detached worktree, same
    full-suite load), so none is caused by this change. Four are
    genuine pre-existing failures (test_absorbing_zero phytoplankton
    seed rate, test_energy_balance_gate porpoise clupeid majority,
    test_gpu_ecosystem cpu-0.0-True-True, test_currents cuda cloud
    field); three are CUDA/torch.compile flakiness under full-suite GPU
    contention and pass in isolation.

    Also run: one-generation train.py on mareld2 (results/
    tick-smoke-20260922), tools/probes/budget_gate.py,
    tools/viability.py --ticks 60 --seeds 1 --behaviour eat --grid 8*8,
    and a headless xvfb GUI test covering construction, label refresh,
    Apply end to end, save/reload, an exact 12 -> 6 -> 12 round trip and
    rejection of an out-of-range value. The GUI test wrote to scratch
    copies; the real fg_library.yaml was never modified.

98 TEST SUITE CLEANUP (2026-09-28)

98.1 Starting point
    Section 97.8 recorded 625 passed, 15 skipped, 7 failed at HEAD
    (ddec80e), of which four were called genuine and three CUDA
    flakiness. All seven are now accounted for: two were numerical
    defects, three were one suite-level defect, and two were
    parameter decisions the user has now made. Suite after this
    section: 633 passed, 15 skipped, 0 failed, in 50 s.

98.2 Fix 1 - the cloud current lost precision as the tick grew

    Symptom: test_currents::
    test_cloud_field_is_spatial_bounded_and_matches_batched_worlds
    [cuda] failed with 1298 of 5244 elements outside rtol 3e-6, max
    absolute difference 4.1e-6. Measured per tick, CPU matched the
    NumPy reference bit for bit at every tick, and CUDA matched to
    ~1 ULP (1e-7) at ticks 0, 1, 20 and 99 - and only at tick 10000
    did it diverge. The tolerance was not the problem.

    Cause: current_direction_fractions sampled the noise lattice at
    (x - tick/period) / scale in float32. The offset is in GRID
    cells and grows without bound: at tick 10000 with period 13 it
    is 769.2, where one float32 ULP is 6.1e-5, so the mantissa is
    spent on the offset and only ~14 bits are left for the position
    INSIDE a lattice cell - the quantity the smoothstep and the two
    lerps actually use. Two devices then round the same field
    differently, and on either device the field freezes into steps
    as the tick grows. period divides the tick, so a short period
    reaches that regime inside an ordinary run.

    Fix: split the offset. Let u = (x - offset)/scale be the true
    lattice coordinate, m = floor(offset/scale) the whole lattice
    cells in it and s = offset - m*scale the remainder below one
    cell. Then u = v - m with v = (x - s)/scale, so floor(u) =
    floor(v) - m and frac(u) = frac(v): the large part moves into
    the int64 lattice index, where it is exact, and the coordinate
    stays small. _current_noise takes a new `shift` argument for
    that index; the detail octave samples at 2u and therefore
    shifts by 2m. The scalar offset is computed in float64 so the
    remainder is not born rounded.

    After: CUDA matches the reference to 9e-8 at ticks 10000, 1e5,
    1e6 and 1e7 - i.e. tick-independent, the same as tick 0.

    Effect on the field: bit-identical while the offset is below
    one lattice cell (with scale 7.5 / period 13 the first change
    is at tick 100), and at most 1.4e-5 different after that, on
    fractions bounded by strength/2. That difference is the
    round-off being removed, not added. The one-cell-per-period
    scroll identity holds exactly in both versions.

    New test: test_cloud_keeps_its_sub_cell_resolution_at_a_large
    _tick. Twelve sub-cell scroll steps (period/2000) must give
    twelve distinct fields at tick 0 AND at tick 1e6. The old code
    gave 12/12 at tick 0 and 3/12 at 1e6, so the test states the
    defect directly and needs no GPU.

98.3 Fix 2 - the GPU/reference parity tolerance was a fixed number
     for a divergence that compounds

    Symptom: test_gpu_ecosystem::test_full_ticks_match_reference
    [cpu-0.0-True-True] failed on one element of 80, rel 4.89e-5
    against rtol 4e-5.

    Measurement before touching anything: the worst cell diverges
    by 1.4e-7 (~1 ULP) at tick 0, stays there for seven ticks, then
    grows by roughly 3x per tick to 4.9e-5 at tick 11. Throughout,
    the action probabilities of the two engines stay identical to
    6e-8 and the biomasses to ~1e-6 relative, so no branch has
    flipped: it is float32 state drift through a nonlinear map, not
    a mirror bug. The pair-visibility fixture was already known to
    do the same faster (its docstring records 1e-7 -> 6e-5 over six
    ticks), which is why that test runs six ticks and not twelve.

    A single constant bound therefore has to be set by the last
    tick of the longest fixture and is blind for the first ones -
    where a real formula difference shows up at once and three
    orders of magnitude larger. _assert_parity now uses
    min(3e-4, 1e-6 * 3**tick) for the observation, biomass and
    reserve comparisons: ULP-tight at tick 0, growing at the rate
    the divergence actually grows, capped where the measurements
    level off. The action comparison is left constant, because
    probabilities are recomputed from the state each tick and do
    not accumulate.

98.4 Fix 3 - the three "CUDA flakiness" failures were one bug

    test_gpu_trainer::test_cuda_execution_matches_eager_and_replay
    _advances[stability1-compile] and [stability1-compile-graph]
    and test_gpu_visual::test_visual_cli_renders_and_preserves
    _training[cuda-compile-graph] failed only in the full run and
    passed on their own. The error was not numerical:
    torch._dynamo.exc.FailOnRecompileLimitHit.

    torch.compile budgets recompilations per code object, default
    8. RolloutRunner._tick is one code object and the suite builds
    many runners that differ in shapes, flags and dtypes, so the
    variants accumulate on that one frame across tests. A training
    run compiles a single runner, so the budget is a property of
    the pytest process, not of production.

    New tests/conftest.py: an autouse fixture calling
    torch._dynamo.reset() after every test. Each test gets a clean
    cache and the limit goes back to meaning something per test.
    Side effect: the suite got FASTER, 78 s -> 50 s, because the
    guard chains no longer grow.

98.5 Decision 1 - phytoplankton.seed_rate stays 0.0

    74.11 left this open ("may be a deliberate experiment. Decide
    explicitly"): sections 69.6 and 73.4 set 1e-07 to remove the
    absorbing zero at the base of the food web, and the FG editor
    reset it to 0 twice. USER DECISION 2026-09-28: keep 0.0, i.e.
    no recolonisation floor - a phytoplankton cell that reaches
    exact zero stays there.

    The value now carries a comment block in fg_library.yaml
    saying so. test_phytoplankton_seed_rate_is_enabled_in_the
    _library is replaced by test_phytoplankton_seed_rate_is_a
    _deliberate_choice, which passes on any value above zero and,
    at zero, requires an explanation next to the value in the raw
    YAML. That is the weaker property, but it is the one that
    catches the actual failure mode: the editor rewrites the
    library WITHOUT comments, so the comment vanishing is the
    signal that the zero came from the editor and not from a
    decision. Verified both ways - stripping the comment turns the
    test red, restoring 1e-07 turns it green through the other
    branch.

98.6 Decision 2 - clupeid energy content 9.5 -> 7.5 MJ/kg

    This was open item 92.9 point 2. USER DECISION 2026-09-28:
    pelagic_fish.energy_content 9500 -> 7500 MJ/t, the annual mean
    rather than the autumn peak.

    What it settles: test_live_project_porpoises_need_a_clupeid
    _majority_not_a_pure_diet. The minimum clupeid share in the
    porpoise ration is (need_q - q_gadoids)/(q_clupeid -
    q_gadoids); at 9500 that is (5669 - 4620)/(8550 - 4620) =
    0.267, i.e. the modelled porpoise could very nearly live on
    lean gadoid, against 50-70 % clupeids by mass in the Kattegat
    and Skagerrak stomach data. At 7500 it is 0.492. The window
    0.40-0.70 that the test allows corresponds to an energy
    content of 6800-8050 MJ/t, so 7.5 sits in the middle of it and
    9.5 is far outside.

    What it costs, from tools/probes/budget_gate.py:
        gadoids   -> pelagic_fish  sat_min 0.092 -> 0.117,
                                   need_vis 0.101 -> 0.132 t/cell
        porpoises -> pelagic_fish  sat_min 0.663 -> 0.840,
                                   need_vis 1.966 -> 5.239 t/cell
    exactly the trade 92.9 predicted. The herring field measured by
    tools/probes/herring_field.py settles around 3.3 t/cell and
    falls to 2.3 by tick 1000, so a porpoise living on clupeids
    ALONE now needs more herring than the field carries. The mixed
    ration is what has to pay, and it is the mix the literature
    describes.

    Viability, full rig, same criterion as section 92:
        verdict          NOT VIABLE, normative corner - UNCHANGED
        porpoises        extinct at 1386 (92.6 recorded 1404)
        pelagic_fish     0.001x spawn, drift 1.83 (was 0.0008x,
                         drift 1.82)
        gadoids          extinct at 4573
    So the choice costs the porpoise about 18 ticks out of 1386 and
    changes nothing structural: the three FAILs remain exactly the
    ones 92.6 and 92.9 describe, and 92.9 point 1 (pelagic_fish
    needs the section 74 treatment) is still the next thing.

    CORRECTION to 92.7: that section reports 514 passed, 12 skipped
    for the commit that added this test (21c38db). The library
    carried energy_content 9500 already at that commit, with every
    other porpoise, gadoid and clupeid parameter identical to
    today, so the test was red the day it landed and the quoted
    count did not include it.

98.7 Validation
    python3 -m pytest tests/ -q      633 passed, 15 skipped, 0
                                     failed (twice, plus once with
                                     the GPU files first, to rule
                                     out order dependence)
    python3 train.py --project mareld2.yaml --species pelagic_fish
        --generations 1 --iter-per-gen 1 --workers 2   completed,
                                     checkpoint written to
                                     results/cleanup-smoke-20260928
    tools/probes/budget_gate.py      numbers quoted in 98.6
    tools/probes/herring_field.py    field mean quoted in 98.6
    tools/viability.py               numbers quoted in 98.6, exit 1
                                     as before

99 EXTERNAL FORCES PER FUNCTIONAL GROUP (2026-09-28)

99.1 What was asked
    A checkbox per FG in the FG editor saying whether the group is
    moved by external forces (wind and currents). Until now only
    phytoplankton was: both engines filtered the advection on
    is_decision_maker, so a checkbox on a swimmer would have been a
    promise the tick does not keep. USER DECISION 2026-09-28: every
    FG can be ticked, decision makers included, which means the
    engines change too - and the widget is a checkbox PLUS a share
    field, because current_response is a fraction in [0, 1] and the
    engine scales flux and energy with it.

99.2 The rule is now the parameter, not the kind

    lib/world/functional_group.py
        current_response keeps its [0, 1] validation, but its DEFAULT
        now depends on the group: 1.0 for a non decision maker, 0.0
        for a decision maker. That is exactly the rule the filter
        used to hardcode, so a library written before this option
        behaves identically - phytoplankton drifts, swimmers do not -
        and nothing had to be written into fg_library.yaml to keep
        the current world as it was.

    movement.apply_currents
        the ids list drops "not is_decision_maker" and keeps only
        "current_response > 0".

    lib/gpu/ecosystem.py
        ndm_positions / ndm_index are no longer what advect() uses.
        New drift_positions / drift_index, built from the same
        current_response > 0 rule, and current_response itself is
        built over that set. ndm_index is untouched for its other
        uses.

    A drifting swimmer is advected AFTER apply_movement, i.e. on top
    of the move its policy chose in the same tick. The order is the
    existing one; only the membership changed.

99.3 The local reward had to learn the current

    source_tracking's exactness argument is explicit that everything
    after the movement is cell-wise multiplicative, and named
    apply_currents as safe *because* it only touched non decision
    makers. That sentence stops being true the moment a decision
    maker drifts: the field moves biomass between cells after the
    move shares were recorded, so the destination whose energy is
    divided among its sources is not the cell the move landed in.

    The fix composes rather than approximates. The field is linear
    and uniform within a cell, so one ton left in cell m by the
    movement is worth

        unit(m) = (1 - sum_d phi_d(m)) * u(m)
                  + sum_d phi_d(m) * u(m_d)

    where u = q_end / b_after_current and phi are the field's
    per-direction shares. Pushing u back through phi BEFORE the move
    shares are cashed in restores the identity exactly, and collapses
    to unit = u for a group that does not drift, so the pre-existing
    path is bit-for-bit unchanged.

    New: source_tracking.record_advection(env, ids, fractions),
    called from apply_currents; env._local_advection holds (phi,
    biomass) and is cleared with the rest in begin_tick/end_tick.
    The GPU mirror is advect(..., track=True), which returns the same
    two things re-indexed on the decision makers, and tracked_energy
    takes them as a new optional argument.

    Unchanged: with --migration on the field emigrates across the
    border and is re-injected elsewhere, which no source cell can
    follow. That is the same hole migration already puts in the move
    shares, and the module docstring now says so for both. Without
    migration the movement mask zeroes the border directions for the
    field exactly as it does for a move, so nothing leaks and the
    identity is exact.

99.4 The widget

    fgconfig/fgconfig.py, one ordinary property row in BOTH editors
    (decision makers and non decision makers), just above the initial
    biomass range:

        External Forces (wind/currents; share carried, 0=off)
                                    [0, 1]  [0.6]  ----o------

    It is built by the same _build_ranged_entry call as every other
    numeric property, so it inherits the column layout, the live
    [0, 1] validation and the slider, and it is clamped on apply the
    way maintenance_level is. "0=off" follows the labels already in
    the same editor ("Interference (... 0=off)", "Extinction
    Threshold (... 0=off)").

    HISTORY, because two designs were built and discarded and the
    reasons matter more than the code:

    * The first version was a checkbox in front of the Entry, per the
      original request. It pushed this row's Entry 25 px right of the
      rows above and made it 9 px narrower - the drift row was the
      only one whose column 1 was not a plain
      _build_inline_ranged_entry, so it was the only one that could
      leave the margin.
    * The second put the checkbox in column 0 beside the label, which
      fixed the alignment. The user then made the sharper point: the
      checkbox carries no information the share does not. The library
      stores ONE number and 0 already means off, so the box bought an
      extra widget, a greying rule, a coercion (empty field -> 1.0)
      and four helper methods to say what a 0 says. It would have
      earned its place only by remembering the share across an
      untick, which it did not do and which the library could not
      persist anyway. USER DECISION 2026-09-28: remove it.

    What survives from all that is one helper, and it is the one that
    was actually load bearing:

    _effective_current_response(config, is_dm)
        A MISSING key is not 0. It means the FunctionalGroup default,
        1.0 for a non decision maker and 0.0 for a decision maker.
        Without resolving it on load the editor would show an empty
        field for every group in a library written before this option,
        and the generic apply path turns an empty field into 0.0 - so
        editing anything at all about phytoplankton would silently
        anchor it. The test below fails with 0.0 when this helper is
        bypassed, which is how that was confirmed rather than assumed.

99.5 Tests

    tests/test_currents.py +2
      * a decision maker with current_response 0.25 / 1.0 is advected,
        east and south, mass conserving, energy following biomass, and
        the CPU and GPU engines agree tensor for tensor.
      * the default rule, as four FunctionalGroup constructions plus
        an end-to-end check that an untouched fixture leaves its
        swimmer exactly where it was and that drift_positions ==
        ndm_positions there.

    tests/test_local_reward.py +4
      * a decision maker only drifts when it responds (guards the
        next test against being vacuous),
      * mass balance holds while a decision maker drifts,
      * the fixture actually varies energy per ton (see below),
      * a drifting cell that rests still scores ratio 1.

    tests/test_gpu_local_reward.py +1 and one parametrisation
      * the mass-balance test now also runs with drift 0.7,
      * rollout fitness matches the CPU trainer's while drifting, at
        response 0.4 and 1.0, which is the end-to-end check that both
        trackers compose the field the same way.

    WATCH OUT - the first version of the mass-balance test passed
    with the composition deleted. The fixture used a uniform reserve
    fill, so the end-of-tick energy per ton was the same in every
    cell, the sum telescoped whatever the allocation did, and the
    assertion could not see a misplaced share at all. The test now
    fills the reserve unevenly and a companion test asserts that the
    resulting spread is real (ptp/mean > 0.05). Verified by deleting
    the composition in each engine in turn: 1 CPU failure and 8 GPU
    failures appear, and disappear when it is restored.

    Suite: 651 passed, 15 skipped, 0 failed (was 633 after 98).

99.6 Validation
    python3 -m pytest tests/ -q                   651 passed
    train.py --currents on                        1 generation, ok
    train.py --currents on --local_reward         1 generation, ok
    headless xvfb GUI run against SCRATCH copies  21 checks:
        phytoplankton shows 1, benthic_community 0, pelagic_fish
        (no key at all in the library) 0; 0.6 + Apply writes 0.6 and
        survives a reload; 1.5 clamps to 1.0; 0 writes 0.0; a library
        entry with the key DELETED shows 1 and is still 1.0 after an
        Apply, i.e. the silent-anchoring trap; every Entry in column 1
        of both editors shares one left margin and one width and the
        share Entry is among them; and FunctionalGroup reads back
        1.0 / 0.0 / 0.0 from the file the GUI wrote.

        Two of these checks were vacuous when first written, in the
        same way 99.5 records for the mass balance, and both were
        found by deleting the code under test rather than by reading:
          * the layout check walked only the DIRECT children of each
            column-1 container, and in the broken layout the Entry
            sat one frame deeper - so the row it was about was
            skipped and the rest agreed trivially. It now searches at
            any depth and asserts separately that the share Entry was
            found. Against the old layout it reports x=[512, 537],
            widths=[152, 161] and fails.
          * bypassing _effective_current_response turns the missing-key
            checks red with 0.0, as it should.

        The real fg_library.yaml was never touched.

99.7 Docs
    CURRENTS.md said "It does not apply to decision makers. All non
    decision makers participate" - both were already stale (the
    per-FG current_response predates this section) and are now
    replaced by a section on which groups the field carries, the
    default rule and the local-reward guarantee.

100 ROLLING HEATMAP NORMALISATION TOGGLE (2026-09-28)

100.1 Why
    Section 94 made the heatmap colour reference the rollout-start
    per-cell maximum, on explicit request, and removed the frame
    dependent maximum entirely. That is the right default: declining
    biomass darkens, and two ticks - or two FGs - can be compared by
    eye. It is also the wrong answer in one case, and the case is not
    rare here: a field that collapses to a thousandth of its start
    goes black, and its spatial structure goes with it. The two
    references answer different questions and neither is always the
    one being asked, so this is a toggle rather than a reversal of 94.

100.2 What it does
    lib/viz/pygame_viz.py, one flag and one branch:

        fixed (default)  reference = rollout-start per-cell max
        rolling ('n')    reference = the max of the frame being drawn

    The display-scale slider multiplies whichever reference is in use,
    log mode ('l') applies on top of either, and the colourbar always
    prints the reference in use - so a frame can still be read without
    knowing which mode produced it, which is what keeps the rolling
    mode honest.

    Key 'n', beside 'l' (log heatmap) and 'r' (log plot), AND a
    clickable button in the status bar reading "norm: fixed [n]" /
    "norm: rolling [n]", green while rolling, styled like the plot's
    Live button.

    The button was not in the first version and had to be: the user
    started a training run and reported seeing no setting for the
    normalisation, which was exactly right. A key is not a setting if
    nothing on screen mentions it, the module docstring is the only
    place the keys are documented (no in-app legend, no README
    section), and the status bar only showed "hm:rolling" once the
    mode was already on - i.e. nothing at all in the default state.
    The natural neighbour would have been the biomass-scale slider,
    but _draw_status_bar draws that in INFERENCE mode only, so during
    training there was no display control on screen whatsoever. The
    status bar's first row is drawn in every mode, so the button lives
    there, right aligned, falling back to just after the status text if
    the window is too narrow to right align it - never hidden, never on
    top of the text. Measured across 1, 2, 6 and 8 FGs (windows 905 to
    1563 px): no overlap, never off screen.

    ONE class, six entry points: train.py, train_gpu.py (via api.py),
    inference.py, simple_inference.py and manual_play.py all construct
    the same LiveVisualizer, so all of them get it.

    Deliberately NOT part of the frame snapshot. _capture_frame stores
    heatmap_vmax0 because that is data about the rollout; the toggle is
    a view setting, so it can be flipped in the middle of a playback,
    and during playback the rolling reference is read from the REPLAYED
    frame's array (swapped in by _swap_in_frame) rather than the live
    one.

    No CLI flag: 'l' and 'r' have none either, and the toggle survives
    a new rollout, so one keypress lasts the session. Adding one would
    touch all six entry points for a setting that is already one key.

100.3 Tests
    tests/test_viz_normalisation.py, 7 cases, all asserting the PIXELS
    the viewer blitted rather than the reference it computed - the
    contract is about colours - by reading the rendered surface under
    SDL's dummy driver and comparing against the viewer's own LUT:
      * fixed is the default and a tenfold collapse reads as a tenfold
        collapse,
      * 'n' gives the same collapsed field the full colour range back,
        and the quarter cell sits a quarter up the gradient rather than
        a fortieth - i.e. the structure the fixed reference flattened,
      * growth above the start reference saturates when fixed and does
        not when rolling,
      * the display-scale slider multiplies both references (checked at
        a tick where the two differ, so the case is distinguishable),
      * an all-zero frame is black in both modes, with no division by
        the zero reference,
      * 'l' and 'n' compose,
      * playback normalises by the replayed frame, and the toggle is
