    does not move (17.3 -> 17.5). The hunger gate fixes per-capita
    production, so a larger food supply buys more grazers rather than
    faster ones - zoo biomass goes to ~5x its spawn level. 111's
    shortfall against the literature band is therefore NOT what this
    fixes; what it fixes is the food web underneath.

    pelagic_fish stays at 0.000 in every arm. Pre-existing in the frozen
    greedy arm (73, 74), untouched by any of this.

113.7 Recommendation
    Set `phytoplankton.growth_rate = 0.125`, restoring the pre-63.3
    value on literature grounds rather than historical ones: mid-band
    for Eppley at 10-12 C minus unmodelled microzooplankton grazing.
    Leave `seasonal_amplitude` at 0.0 - section 112.4 measured 63.3's
    0.7 recommendation and it makes the mean supply worse.

    Consequences to accept with it:
      * zooplankton settles at ~5x its `mareld2.yaml` spawn level.
        WITHDRAWN - see 113.9. Recalibrating spawn is the wrong move
        and the 5x is a symptom worth keeping visible, not fitting
        away. It also makes this recommendation conditional.
      * Every checkpoint in `results/` becomes stale in substance -
        no shape changes, so nothing is invalidated formally.
      * Sections 109.5, 111.4 and 112.5 each defend 0.0375 as
        deliberately calibrated. They are superseded by this section
        and carry pointers to it.

    NOT DONE HERE: the instruction was to investigate.
    APPLIED by section 121, after 120.3 measured it in the
    mass-balanced world.

113.9 WITHDRAWN from 113.7: "recalibrate the zooplankton spawn level"
    113.7 listed spawn recalibration as a consequence to accept. That
    was wrong, and checking the numbers turns it into a better finding
    than the one it was hiding.

    What the spawn level is: `initial_biomass_min/max` (and
    `inference_initial_biomass`) in `mareld2.yaml`, in tons for the
    60x60 reference grid, scaled at load time by cell count. It matters
    beyond the starting point because ARS measures fitness as
    `delta_b = log((bh + eps)/(b0 + eps))` - b0 IS the spawn level - so
    a spawn far from equilibrium pays a policy for the world relaxing
    on its own. That is the mechanism that made "recalibrate" sound
    reasonable.

    But the spawn levels are not arbitrary numbers waiting to be fitted
    to the model. On 60x60:

      phytoplankton     80000 t   (93 % of K = 24 t/cell x 3600)
      zooplankton       35000 t
      zoo : phyto at spawn                     0.44 : 1

    Phytoplankton spawning at 93 % of its own carrying capacity, and a
    grazer standing stock at 0.44 of its food's, is what a temperate
    shelf sea looks like. Those are biological statements. Moving them
    so the model's equilibrium stops looking odd is exactly the
    inversion section 111 rejected for growth_rate, applied to the
    spawn file instead.

    What the same arithmetic says about the equilibrium:

      phyto growth_rate   phyto eq    zoo eq     zoo : phyto
      0.0375 (current)      9200 t    30275 t      3.3 : 1
      0.125  (113.7)       26160 t   177765 t      6.8 : 1

    Both are inverted, and the proposed value makes it worse - at 0.125
    the zooplankton standing stock is 2x the entire carrying capacity of
    its own food. Inverted pelagic pyramids exist, but not at 7:1 in a
    productive coastal system, and not against a grazer that outweighs
    the maximum possible biomass of what it eats.

    So 113.7 is not the clean win 113.6's gadoid row makes it look like.
    Raising phytoplankton production does repair the food web above
    zooplankton, and the double count in 113.1-113.5 is real
    independently of this, but something in the zooplankton-
    phytoplankton pair is still wrong in a way neither this section nor
    109-112 has located: nothing in the tick's growth term conserves
    mass between ingested prey and added predator biomass - biomass
    grows by `g * surplus` whatever was eaten, with energy acting only
    as a gate through s_X - so a grazer can accumulate standing stock
    its food could not have produced. Whether that is the explanation
    here is not established, and Strategi.pdf should be read on the
    point before anyone changes it.

    Concrete follow-up that costs nothing: the zoo:phyto standing-stock
    ratio is a cheap plausibility check and no arm of the viability rig
    reports it. Every number in 109-113 was read off per-FG levels
    against spawn, which cannot see a ratio going wrong. Adding it
    would have caught this at section 109.

113.8 Sources
    Eppley, R.W. (1972) Temperature and phytoplankton growth in the
      sea. Fish. Bull. 70:1063-1085.
    Calbet, A. (2001) Mesozooplankton grazing effect on primary
      production: a global comparative analysis in marine ecosystems.
      Limnol. Oceanogr. 46:1824-1830.
    Calbet, A. & Landry, M.R. (2004) Phytoplankton growth,
      microzooplankton grazing, and carbon cycling in marine systems.
      Limnol. Oceanogr. 49:51-57.
    The HELCOM figure 63.3 cites is not identified in this repo and was
    not located.

114 THE GROWTH TERM IS NOT MASS-BALANCED, BY DESIGN, AND IT SHOWS (2026-09-29)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

114.1 What Strategi.pdf says
    Read on the point raised in 113.9. Under "Berakna nettotillvaxt
    efter rorelse" the document gives, for functional groups whose
    energy is modelled:

      s_X(c) = E_X(c) / ME_X
      q_X(c) = s_X(c) - u_X                    u_X = maintenance level
      B_X(c,t+1) = B*_X(c) * (1 + MG_X * q_X(c)) + impact_delta,
                   clipped to >= 0

    with the heading note "Tillvaxten begransas av populationens
    mattnad" - growth is limited by the population's SATIATION.

    That is exactly what `_apply_decision_maker_population_change`
    implements. The code is faithful to the design; the absence of a
    mass balance between prey eaten and predator biomass added is in
    the design, not in the implementation. Energy is a GATE on growth,
    never a currency spent to buy biomass. Nothing anywhere in the
    document ties `MG_X * q_X` to what was ingested.

    (Swedish quotations in this section are transliterated - a, a, o for
    the accented vowels - because this file is ASCII only. They will not
    string-match the PDF.)

    Noted per the user: the document is a draft of an initial model to
    develop further, not a specification to conform to. AGENTS.md still
    calls it "authoritative for the mathematics", which no longer
    matches how it is to be used.

114.2 One thing the document leaves open, and the code picks well
    The growth step says what happens to B and is silent on R_X and
    E_X. Two readings: keep total reserve R constant, so E = R/B falls
    as biomass grows (added biomass dilutes the reserve), or keep E
    constant, so R grows with B (energy created from nothing). The code
    takes the first, which is the conservative one, and it is the only
    implicit cost the growth term pays.

114.3 Measured: the growth term produces about twice the mass it eats
    `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py`, 60x60, 3000 ticks, means over
    the last 300, frozen greedy, colocated, `--mortality on`, zooplankton
    at its section 111 value 0.0913.

      phyto growth_rate           0.0375 (current)   0.125 (113.7)
      zoo standing stock             26327 t            176917 t
      phyto standing stock           10206 t             26228 t
      zoo : phyto                     2.58 : 1            6.75 : 1
      phyto grazed                   116.9 t/tick        859.7 t/tick
      zoo produced by growth         231.0 t/tick       1815.3 t/tick
      IMPLIED gross efficiency       197.6 %             211.2 %

    Cross-check on the production figure: at equilibrium production must
    equal total losses, and the losses sum to 340.0 and 1885.3 t/tick
    respectively - within 4 % of the growth-term estimate, so the number
    is not an artefact of sampling the state after the tick.

114.4 What the ceiling actually is, and the one assumption in it
    `interactions.py` builds `energy_gain = prey_energy_content *
    assimilation_factor`. For zooplankton eating phytoplankton that is
    2000 MJ/t * 0.65 = 1300 MJ per ton of phytoplankton grazed.

    The other half of the ratio needs a stated assumption.
    `energy_content` is a PREY-side parameter: what a predator gains per
    ton of this FG when it eats it. The library has no separate
    construction cost for biomass, so `energy_content` is the only
    figure available for "what a ton of this group embodies", and using
    it as the cost of building one is a modelling assumption, not a
    library fact. Taking it that way - zooplankton at 4500 MJ/t - one
    ton grazed can become at most

      1300 / 4500 = 28.9 %

    of a ton of zooplankton, BEFORE any maintenance cost. A reader who
    rejects the assumption still has to explain the 198 %: no assumption
    makes 2 tons of grazer out of 1 ton of plant.
    Measured 198-211 % against a 28.9 % ceiling is a factor 6.9-7.3.
    Copepod gross growth efficiency in the literature is 20-40 %, i.e.
    the ceiling is roughly the right order and the model is nowhere near
    it.

    The growth term is therefore not merely unconstrained by mass - it
    is running well past what the energy bookkeeping in the same tick
    says is possible. The two halves of the tick disagree with each
    other.

114.5 The balance is closed by starvation, which makes it a futile cycle
    Where the created biomass goes, per tick, at the current
    phytoplankton value:

      starvation   274.2 t   (81 % of all losses)
      natural       65.8 t
      predation      0.0 t   (pelagic_fish is at ~0 in this arm)

    The 81 % is ARM-SPECIFIC and should not be quoted on its own: in
    the frozen greedy arm pelagic_fish sits at ~0, so zooplankton has
    no predator and starvation is the only thing left that can close
    the balance. Under a trained policy with pelagic_fish alive,
    predation takes part of that share. The robust number is the 198 %
    production-to-grazing ratio; the split is what this arm does with
    it.

    The growth step creates biomass the food could not support, the
    starvation branch destroys it again at `starve_rate = 0.2`, and the
    two very nearly cancel. The standing stock that survives this churn
    is what makes the zoo:phyto ratio inverted - 2.58:1 now, 6.75:1 at
    113.7's phytoplankton value.

    This also explains why section 111's "delivered P/B" saturates at
    ~17/yr no matter how high the ceiling goes, and why it does not move
    when the food supply triples (113.6). That metric measures the
    positive part of the growth term only. It is gross production in the
    growing cells, not net production, and with 81 % of losses being
    starvation the two are far apart. Every `realPB` figure in 109-113
    should be read that way.

114.6 Pre-existing, and confirmed as the 113.9 suspicion
    197.6 % at the current phytoplankton value against 211.2 % at the
    proposed one: the defect is essentially scale-invariant and is not
    caused by anything in sections 108-113. 113.9 raised it as an
    unestablished possibility and said to read Strategi.pdf first. It is
    now established, and the document is where it comes from.

    It also revises 113.7 further. Raising phytoplankton production
    still repairs the food web above zooplankton and the double count in
    113.1-113.5 is real regardless - but the change would be feeding a
    growth term that converts mass at 7x the possible rate, so a bigger
    supply mostly buys a bigger futile cycle.

114.7 What a mass-balanced version would need
    The minimal change that keeps the document's gate semantics and adds
    conservation is a cap, not a new equation:

      dB = min( B * MG_X * q_X ,
                (assimilated energy this tick - maintenance)
                / energy_content_X )

    i.e. growth is still gated by satiation, but can never exceed the
    biomass the tick's own assimilated energy can build. Note the
    divisor: `energy_content_X`, not `ME_X` - `ME_X` is
    `max_energy_reserve` (450 MJ/t for zooplankton), the per-ton RESERVE
    ceiling, a different quantity from the energy a ton of biomass
    embodies, and confusing the two is easy because both are MJ/t. This
    also inherits 114.4's assumption that `energy_content` doubles as a
    construction cost; a cleaner model would give each FG its own.

    At the current values the cap is 116.9 t/tick * 1300 MJ/t
    / 4500 MJ/t = 33.8 t/tick before maintenance, against the 231
    t/tick actually produced.

    Consequences to expect, none of them measured: every DM's
    `growth_rate` becomes a ceiling that is no longer reachable, so the
    section 111 calibration would have to be redone against realised
    production rather than the anabolic ceiling; the starvation churn
    largely disappears; standing stocks fall, probably by a lot; and
    every checkpoint becomes meaningless because the ecology changes
    shape rather than scale.

    This alters the core growth equation of Strategi.pdf. Not done, not
    a decision to take from a probe result.

114.8 Other deviations from the document noticed while reading
    * `u_X`: the document says "t.ex. 0,3"; every DM in
      fg_library.yaml uses `maintenance_level: 0.5`. AGENTS.md also
      states 0.3 as the default. Not investigated here.
    * "all predation maskas bort om energinivan s_X ar over 0.8" - the
      0.8 satiation gate is from the document, and `satiation_scale`
      (section 69) is a per-FG override of it.
    * "Naturlig mortalitet kan bakas in i tillvaxt" - the document
      permits folding natural mortality into the growth term. The code
      keeps them separate, which is what sections 110 and 111 concluded
      it should, so the two readings agree on the outcome.

115 FOUR WAYS TO MASS-BALANCE THE GROWTH TERM, ONE OF THEM MEASURED (2026-09-29)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

115.1 Where the leak is, exactly
    Worth pinning before proposing anything, because it is one line.
    Energy enters and leaves in `_apply_movement`: intake is added from
    `temp_energy_gains`, action costs are subtracted, and the result is
    clipped, `new_reserve = clip(r_total, 0, b_total * ME)`. Then
    `population_change` adds biomass and **does not touch the reserve**.

    So new biomass arrives carrying an implied `energy_content` (4500
    MJ/t for zooplankton) that was never withdrawn from anywhere. The
    only thing that happens to the reserve is dilution - the same total
    R spread over more B - which lowers `s_X` but destroys no energy.
    Growth is free.

    The ME clip is a second, opposite leak (energy above ME*B is
    discarded) but it barely binds here: the satiation gate stops intake
    at s = 0.8 and zooplankton sits near s = 0.63, so the clip is not
    what section 114 measured.

    Every option below is a different answer to "what should growth pay,
    and to whom".

115.2 Option A - energy as currency: charge the reserve. MEASURED.
    Keep the document's equation as the WISH and make it a purchase:

      E_avail = max(0, R - u_X * ME_X * B)      reserve above maintenance
      dB      = min(B * MG_X * q_X, E_avail / ec_X)
      R      -= dB * ec_X                       <-- the point

    The cap alone is what 114.7 proposed and it is NOT the fix; the
    debit is. Without it the reserve refills freely and s_X only falls
    by dilution, which is far weaker: at zooplankton's equilibrium the
    debit would remove ~123 MJ per ton from a reserve of ~283 MJ/t,
    a 43 % drawdown per tick, against a 2.7 % dilution.

    Prototyped in `tools/probes/growth_debit_prototype.py`, monkey-patched
    for every DM, 60x60, 3000 ticks, means over the last 300, frozen
    greedy, colocated, `--mortality on`, zooplankton at 0.0913:

      arm                       eff.    starv/tick   zoo      phyto   gadoids
      baseline                 343.8 %    309.6      0.74x    0.14x    0.24x
      debit, phyto 0.0375       19.2 %      0.0      0.11x    0.02x    0.31x
      debit, phyto 0.125        19.7 %      2.0      0.36x    0.02x    0.48x
      debit, phyto 0.25         21.4 %     31.8      0.91x    0.03x    1.35x

    (eff. = zooplankton production / phytoplankton grazed; ceiling 28.9 %
    per 114.4. Multiples are of the `mareld2.yaml` spawn level. The
    baseline row reads 343.8 % rather than 114.3's 197.6 % because it
    measures production as total losses rather than the growth term's
    positive part - the two bracket the same defect.)

    Three things this settles:
      * Conservation is restored and STAYS restored: 19-21 % across a
        7x range of phytoplankton supply, always under the 28.9 %
        ceiling, and inside the 20-40 % copepod GGE band.
      * The futile cycle disappears. Starvation goes from 310 t/tick to
        0. Section 114.5's churn was entirely an artefact of paying for
        nothing and then destroying it.
      * Gadoids IMPROVE at every phytoplankton value, 0.24x -> 0.31x
        with nothing else changed. Less zooplankton, but real
        zooplankton.

115.3 Option A introduces a hard bound that has to be checked
    With a debit, the cap `E_avail/ec` and the wish `B*MG*q` are both
    linear in q, so their ratio is a constant:

      cap / wish = ME_X / (ec_X * MG_X)

    which means `growth_rate` above `ME_X / ec_X` can NEVER be reached,
    whatever it is set to. Per FG:

      FG               ME      ec    ME/ec   growth_rate   bound binds?
      zooplankton     450    4500    0.100      0.0913        no, barely
      pelagic_fish   2400    7500    0.320      0.0040        no
      gadoids        2500    5500    0.455      0.0025        no
      porpoises      2800    8500    0.329      0.0010        no
      seals          5200   10000    0.520      0.0010        no
      seabirds       3400    7000    0.486      0.0010        no

    Zooplankton is at 91 % of its own bound and every other DM is two
    orders below. So section 111's literature value survives Option A
    by a margin of 9 %, which is luck rather than design, and any future
    upward revision of it would hit a ceiling set by
    `max_energy_reserve / energy_content` rather than by biology.

115.4 Option B - consumption-driven growth. The one to prefer.
    Drop the energy ratio from the growth term entirely and define
    production from what was eaten:

      dB = K_X * Q_X(c)  -  losses

    where Q_X(c) is this tick's assimilated intake in the cell and K_X
    is the gross growth efficiency. The reserve is demoted to what it is
    good at - driving hunger, the satiation gate and starvation - and
    stops pretending to be a construction budget.

    Why this is better than A even though A already works:
      * `growth_rate` as an "anabolic ceiling" has no literature value.
        Sections 109, 110 and 111 are three attempts to calibrate it and
        the third one had to set it from P/B, which is a realised
        population statistic being read onto a maximum. K_X does have a
        literature value, measured directly: copepod gross growth
        efficiency 20-40 %, fish 10-30 %.
      * Conservation is structural rather than enforced. There is no
        cap, no debit and no bound like 115.3's to audit.
      * A converges on what B would declare. The prototype settles at
        19-21 % by itself; Option B would write 0.25 in the library and
        be done. A DISCOVERS the number, B DECLARES it.

    Cost: growth becomes impossible in a tick with no intake, even for
    a well-fed animal with a full reserve, which is wrong for anything
    larger than a copepod. The fix is to let reserve drawdown count as
    intake: `dB = K_X * (Q_X + max(0, drawdown))`, which is Option C in
    miniature.

    Migration path: implement A, confirm it lands where B predicts (it
    does, 19-21 % against a literature 20-40 %), then replace the
    parameter. A is the experiment, B is the model.

115.5 Option C - two pools: structure and reserve
    Make the split explicit instead of implicit. Biomass becomes
    structure S; the reserve R is a separate state with its own
    dynamics; assimilation fills R; a fraction of reserve mobilisation
    pays maintenance and the remainder buys S at a fixed cost per ton.
    This is the DEB kappa-rule reduced to two pools.

    What it buys over A and B:
      * Starvation stops being a separate branch with its own
        `starve_rate`. An animal starves because R runs out, which is
        what starvation IS. One parameter fewer and a mechanism instead
        of a rule.
      * Condition (`s_X`) becomes a real state variable rather than a
        ratio to a cap, so the satiation gate and the hunger term stop
        being two views of the same number.
      * Reproduction can be separated from somatic growth, which is what
        section 112 found the model cannot currently express.

    Cost: it is a rewrite of the DM branch and of the energy update in
    `_apply_movement`, it invalidates every checkpoint by changing the
    observation semantics of energy, and DEB parameters are harder to
    source than K_X. Not a next step; the right destination if the model
    is going to carry more biology.

115.6 Option D - not a fix, but the companion any of them needs
    `ME_X / ec_X` is 0.1 for zooplankton: a FULL reserve funds one tenth
    of a ton of new biomass per ton standing. Whatever option is chosen,
    that ratio is what decides how much growth the energy state can
    finance, and it was never set with that job in mind -
    `max_energy_reserve` was calibrated as a starvation window
    (`starve_calibration.py`, section 36 territory) and `energy_content`
    as a prey-side value. Under A and C they also become the growth
    budget. Both should be re-derived once, together, against what they
    are being asked to do.

115.7 What none of them fixes, and it is the next question
    In every debited arm phytoplankton settles at 0.02-0.03 of its spawn
    level and the zoo:phyto ratio gets WORSE, 2.3:1 -> 8:1 -> 12:1 as
    the supply rises. That is not a conservation failure any more - it
    is the classic R* result. Zooplankton keeps growing as long as
    per-ton intake exceeds per-ton maintenance, so it grazes
    phytoplankton down to the density where

      f(P) * energy_gain = resting_metabolism * cost_eat

    and that density is very low because zooplankton's maintenance
    (3.5 MJ/t/tick) is cheap against the energy in its food (1300 MJ per
    ton grazed). Fixing the growth term makes this visible instead of
    hiding it behind fabricated biomass.

    So the ranking of what is actually wrong, after 114 and this
    section: (1) growth is not mass-balanced, (2) zooplankton's
    maintenance/intake/energy-content triple sets an implausible R*,
    (3) phytoplankton's growth_rate is net of grazing (113). All three
    are independent and (1) has to go first, because the other two
    cannot be measured through a growth term that fabricates mass.

115.8 Nothing changed
    Design note only. No file in `lib/` or `fgconfig/` was modified; the
    prototype monkey-patches `_apply_decision_maker_population_change`
    inside `tools/probes/growth_debit_prototype.py`.

116 CLI FLAG --mass-balance: THE GROWTH TERM PAYS FOR WHAT IT ADDS (2026-09-29)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

116.1 What it does
    Option A of section 115, implemented behind a flag so it can be A/B
    tested against the legacy term without committing to it. Off by
    default; off is bit-identical to the pre-change engine.

    The growth WISH is unchanged and still exactly what Strategi.pdf
    specifies, `B * MG_X * q_X`. What changes is that it is now a
    purchase:

      E_avail = max(0, R - u_X * ME_X * B)
      dB      = min(B * MG_X * q_X, E_avail / energy_content_X)
      R      -= dB * energy_content_X

    Only the POSITIVE part of the delta is touched. Starvation is a loss,
    not a purchase, and its branch is byte-identical with the flag on -
    asserted by `tests/test_mass_balance.py`.

116.2 Semantics worth knowing before using it
    * The DEBIT is the mechanism, not the cap. Section 114.7 proposed the
      cap alone; that is not enough, because without the debit the
      reserve refills freely and `s_X` falls only by dilution, an order
      of magnitude weaker (115.2).
    * `max_energy_reserve / energy_content` becomes a hard ceiling on the
      reachable `growth_rate`, since cap and wish are both linear in
      `q_X`. Zooplankton sits at 0.0913 against a bound of 0.100 - 91 %
      of it. Every other DM is two orders below its own bound (115.3).
    * `energy_content` is a prey-side parameter being used as a
      construction cost. The library has no separate figure, so this is
      an assumption the flag inherits from 114.4, not a fact.
    * An FG with `energy_content = 0` has no definable price and keeps
      the legacy term. That is a configuration bug the growth step is
      not the place to diagnose.

116.3 Where it lives
    Flag defined ONCE, in `population_change.add_mass_balance_argument`,
    for the same reason `--mortality_multiplier` is (88.3): a flag that
    changes the tick must not mean two things on two parsers.

      lib/environments/ecosystem_env/population_change.py
          add_mass_balance_argument, DEFAULT_MASS_BALANCE,
          _charge_growth_to_reserve  (the term itself)
      lib/environments/ecosystem_env/environment.py
          EcosystemEnvironment(mass_balance=False) -> env.mass_balance
      lib/gpu/ecosystem.py
          TensorEcosystem.population  (the mirror)
      train.py        MASS_BALANCE global, both env builders, the banner
      lib/runners/training_progress.py
                      recorded in the run config, defaulted for older
                      histories in `comparable_config`, and passed to
                      `build_inference_env`. Missing this last one would
                      have made every progress rollout of a
                      `--mass-balance` run measure the LEGACY ecology,
                      which is the same trap the multiplier fell into.
      inference.py    build_env(mass_balance=...), all three call sites
      tools/viability.py
      lib/gpu/cli.py, lib/gpu/config.py, lib/gpu/visual.py

    `lib/runners/parallel_worker.py` needs no change: the flag travels
    inside the pickled env builder, like every other builder field.
    Verified by a `--workers 2` run rather than by inspection.

116.4 Measured, with the real flag rather than the prototype
    `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py`, 60x60, 3000 ticks, means over
    the last 300, frozen greedy, colocated, `--mortality on`, library
    values (zooplankton 0.0913, phytoplankton 0.0375):

                              legacy        --mass-balance
      zoo standing stock      24124 t          3959 t
      phyto standing stock     8883 t          1448 t
      zoo : phyto              2.72 : 1        2.73 : 1
      phyto grazed            107.4 t/tick     51.5 t/tick
      zoo production          293.8 t/tick      9.9 t/tick
      of which starvation     233.5 t/tick      0.0 t/tick
      gross efficiency        273.7 %          19.2 %
      (ceiling 2000*0.65/4500 = 28.9 %)

    19.2 % against the prototype's 19.2 %, and standing stocks matching
    it to one ton (3959 vs 3958, 1448 vs 1449) - the flag reproduces
    section 115.2 exactly, which is what makes the two independent.

    Note the legacy row is UNSTEADY: the same arm has measured 190.8 %,
    273.7 % and 343.8 % across runs of this probe at 3000 ticks, because
    the fabricate-then-starve cycle never settles. The debited arm gives
    the same number every time. That stability is itself a result.

    The probe's production estimator had to change with the flag. Under
    `--mass-balance` the growth term is capped, so `B*(1-nm)*g*(s-u)` is
    the WISH and not the spend; production is now read off the losses,
    which is exact at equilibrium and agrees with the delta estimate to
    a few per cent when the flag is off.

116.5 The viability corner
    `--spawn colocated --behaviour greedy --mortality on`, 20x20, 7300
    ticks, 3 seeds:

      fg              legacy                  --mass-balance
      gadoids         FAIL below floor        FAIL extinct@3506
      pelagic_fish    FAIL below floor        FAIL below floor
      porpoises       FAIL extinct@212        FAIL extinct@86
      benthic         PASS 0.702              PASS 0.721
      phytoplankton   FAIL 0.065, drift 1.31  FAIL 0.018, drift 1.00
      zooplankton     PASS 0.702, drift 1.18  PASS 0.113, drift 1.00
      overall         NOT VIABLE              NOT VIABLE

    Those are the rig's 3-seed figures, so zooplankton's 0.113 is the
    seed-worst level and not one lucky draw.

    Read honestly: the world gets quantitatively POORER and
    qualitatively more settled. Every level falls, gadoids tip from
    asymptotic-zero into a recorded extinction, and zooplankton lands at
    0.113 against the criterion's 0.1 floor - one more step down and it
    fails too. But the drift column goes to 1.00 for both plankton
    groups: the system is stationary, which it was not before. The
    biomass that disappeared was the biomass that was never paid for.

    The verdict does not change and is still driven by the same
    pre-existing failures (73, 74, 90-92).

116.6 What this does NOT do
    It does not make the world viable, and it is not expected to. Per
    115.7 the ranking is (1) growth not mass-balanced - this flag, (2)
    zooplankton's maintenance/intake/energy-content triple setting an
    implausible R*, (3) phytoplankton's growth_rate being net of grazing
    (113). This is (1) only. (2) is what the low phytoplankton level in
    116.5 is measuring and it is untouched.

    It also does not settle the design. Option B of section 115 -
    consumption-driven growth with a literature `K_X` - remains the one
    to prefer, and this flag is the experiment that says where it should
    land: 19.2 %, inside the 20-40 % copepod band.

116.7 Tests
    `tests/test_mass_balance.py`, 19 cases: default-off and off being the
    legacy term; the debit equalling `dB * energy_content`; the cap
    landing the reserve exactly on the maintenance line and never below
    zero; the `ME/EC` bound at three growth rates; `energy_content = 0`
    falling back; the starvation branch bit-identical across the flag;
    and CPU/GPU parity for both flag states at two growth rates and both
    `energy_content` values, since `energy_content = 0` is the one branch
    where the two implementations differ structurally - the reference
    returns early, the mirror guards with `torch.where(ec > 0, ...)`.

    The GPU parity case was verified to bite by disabling the mirror -
    two of four cases turn red on their own.

    Suite: 757 passed, 12 skipped (was 734/12). One-generation training
    run with `--mortality on --mass-balance --workers 2` completed.

116.8 Checkpoints
    Not invalidated formally - no observation shape, FG set, grid layout
    or Observability entry moved - but a policy trained with the flag on
    and run with it off, or the reverse, is being evaluated in a
    different world. The flag is recorded in the `training_progress`
    config so a run says which one it was.

117 A/B: --mass-balance x PHYTOPLANKTON 0.125. THE RESOURCE LEVEL IS INVARIANT (2026-09-29)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

117.1 Why this 2x2
    Section 113 recommends raising `phytoplankton.growth_rate` from
    0.0375 to 0.125 on the ground that 0.0375 is net of the grazing the
    tick applies. Section 116 implements a mass-balanced growth term
    behind `--mass-balance`. Section 115.7 ranks the two as independent
    defects and predicts that fixing the growth term first will change
    what the phytoplankton change buys. This measures it.

    All four arms: 60x60, 3000 ticks, frozen greedy, colocated,
    `--mortality on`, zooplankton at its library value 0.0913.

117.2 Conservation and throughput
    `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py`, means over the last 300 ticks:

      arm                  zoo t    phyto t  zoo:phyto  grazed  prod   eff.
      legacy    0.0375     28395     9964     2.85:1     100.0  467.8  468 %
      legacy    0.125     176522    26180     6.74:1     861.0 1864.9  217 %
      mass-bal  0.0375      3959     1448     2.73:1      51.5    9.9   19 %
      mass-bal  0.125      12700     1570     8.09:1     171.9   33.9   20 %

    (grazed and prod in t/tick; eff. = production/grazed against the
    28.9 % ceiling of 114.4. Production is read off the losses, the only
    estimator valid under the flag - 116.4.)

    The result is in the phyto column. Under `--mass-balance`, tripling
    phytoplankton's growth rate moves its standing stock from 1448 to
    1570 t - EIGHT PER CENT. What moves is the throughput: grazing
    51.5 -> 171.9 t/tick, +234 %, and zooplankton 3959 -> 12700 t,
    +221 %. Conservation holds either way, 19.2 % and 19.7 %.

    That is the textbook R* result and it is what a mass-balanced
    consumer is supposed to do: the equilibrium resource level is set by
    the CONSUMER's break-even, not by the resource's growth rate. Faster
    primary production buys more flux at the same standing stock.

    The legacy rows do not show it - phytoplankton goes 9964 -> 26180 t,
    +163 % - because a consumer whose growth is decoupled from its
    ingestion does not have a well-defined R* to pin the resource at.
    Finding the invariance only after the growth term is fixed is
    itself a check on the fix.

117.3 The food web
    `tools/probes/zoo_growth_sweep.py`, 3 seeds, ranges over seeds:

      arm                 phy_end      zoo_end     zoo_min      gad_end
      legacy   0.0375   0.069-0.149  0.64-1.16  0.000-0.001  0.10-0.24
      legacy   0.125    0.325-0.337  5.09-5.11  0.049-0.058  0.65-0.98
      mass-bal 0.0375   0.018        0.113      0.001-0.002  0.14-0.32
      mass-bal 0.125    0.020        0.362      0.361-0.362  0.37-0.51

    Two things stand out beyond 117.2.

    First, the OSCILLATION IS GONE. In the mass-balanced 0.125 arm
    zooplankton's trough over the whole 3000 ticks is 0.361 against a
    final-window mean of 0.362 - it never dips below its equilibrium at
    all. The legacy arm at the same phytoplankton value swings from a
    trough of 0.049 to a mean of 5.11, a factor 100. The predator-prey
    cycling that dominated every measurement in sections 109 to 113 was
    the fabricate-then-starve cycle, not ecology.

    Second, the SEED SPREAD collapses. Legacy phy_end spans 0.069-0.149
    across three seeds; the mass-balanced arms read 0.018, 0.018, 0.018
    and 0.113, 0.113, 0.113. A deterministic equilibrium, which is what
    made the legacy arm's 190.8 / 273.7 / 343.8 / 467.7 % efficiency
    spread across runs (116.4) unmeasurable.

    Gadoids improve with the phytoplankton change in BOTH worlds, 0.10-
    0.24 -> 0.65-0.98 legacy and 0.14-0.32 -> 0.37-0.51 mass-balanced.
    The absolute level is lower under the flag because part of the legacy
    gadoid biomass was fed by zooplankton that was never paid for.

117.4 The viability corner
    `--spawn colocated --behaviour greedy --mortality on`, 20x20, 7300
    ticks, 3 seeds. The 0.0375 column is from 116.5:

      fg             phyto 0.0375              phyto 0.125
                   legacy      mass-bal      legacy      mass-bal
      gadoids      FAIL floor  FAIL ext@3506 FAIL floor  FAIL floor
      pelagic      FAIL floor  FAIL floor    FAIL floor  FAIL floor
      porpoises    ext@212     ext@86        ext@229     ext@97
      benthic      PASS 0.702  PASS 0.721    PASS 0.402  PASS 0.634
      phyto        FAIL 0.065  FAIL 0.018    PASS 0.302  FAIL 0.019
      zoo          PASS 0.702  PASS 0.113    PASS 4.891  PASS 0.359
      overall      NOT VIABLE  NOT VIABLE    NOT VIABLE  NOT VIABLE

    Raising phytoplankton RESCUES its own viability criterion in the
    legacy world - 0.065 FAIL to 0.302 PASS - and does nothing for it
    under the flag, 0.018 to 0.019. Same invariance as 117.2, now with a
    verdict attached.

    Under the flag both plankton groups sit at drift 1.00 with
    zooplankton's trough at 0.357 against an equilibrium of 0.359. One
    improvement worth noting: gadoids go from `extinct@3506` back to
    below-floor-but-alive when phytoplankton is raised.

    The overall verdict is NOT VIABLE in all four arms, still driven by
    the same pre-existing failures (73, 74, 90-92).

117.5 What this settles, and what it changes
    * Section 113's recommendation stands but its VALUE changes. Raising
      phytoplankton to 0.125 is a productivity fix, not a standing-stock
      fix: +234 % throughput and roughly double the gadoids, and 8 % on
      phytoplankton's own biomass. It does NOT repair the inverted
      zoo:phyto ratio - it makes it worse, 2.73:1 -> 8.09:1, because
      zooplankton scales with throughput while its food's stock does not.
      113.9's withdrawal of the spawn recalibration was right for a
      better reason than the one it gave.
    * The inversion is now unambiguously defect (2) of 115.7 -
      zooplankton's maintenance / intake / energy-content triple setting
      an R* far below any plausible phytoplankton standing stock. Not
      the growth term, and not phytoplankton's growth rate. Both of the
      other two candidates have now been eliminated by measurement
      rather than by argument.
    * The next lever is therefore that triple. The R* condition is
      f(P) * energy_gain = resting_metabolism * cost_eat, and the
      quantity to check first is `resting_metabolism = 3.5 MJ/t/tick`
      against 1300 MJ per ton grazed: a maintenance cost that cheap
      pins the resource at a refuge level whatever else is done.
      Untested.

117.6 Probe note
    `zoo_growth_sweep.py`'s `realPB` column is the growth-term WISH, not
    production, when `--mass-balance` is on - the reserve caps the term,
    so `growth_rate * <s-u> * 1460` is what was wanted rather than what
    was spent. It reads 0.2-0.3 in the flagged arms, which is the
    surplus collapsing to the maintenance line, not production
    collapsing. The probe now prints that warning itself.
    `growth_mass_balance.py` reads production off the losses and is the
    one to quote. Nothing in the library or in `lib/` changed for this
    section; the phytoplankton value was overridden in memory, except
    for the corner runs, where the library was edited and restored.

118 ZOOPLANKTON resting_metabolism IS ~1/6 OF A COPEPOD'S, AND THE CONSTRAINT THAT SET IT NEVER BOUND (2026-09-29)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

118.1 The number
    `resting_metabolism: 3.5` MJ/t/tick, i.e. 14.0 MJ/t/day, against
    `energy_content: 4500` MJ/t:

      14.0 / 4500 = 0.311 % of body energy per day at rest
      x cost_eat 1.45                = 0.451 %/day while feeding

    Section 117.5 named this triple as the remaining defect. This is the
    metabolism leg of it.

118.2 How it got there: fitted backwards from a starvation window
    `tools/starve_calibration.py` is the calibration of record. It drives
    one isolated cell with `pi_rest = 1` and no prey and reports T1
    (days to drain the reserve from u_X) and T_total (days to 95 %
    biomass loss), against a literature target of 3-30 days for
    zooplankton - "active copepods 3-14 d; lipid-rich up to 30+". At the
    library values it prints

      zooplankton   ME 450   rm 3.5   starve 0.200   T1 16.2 d   T_total 15.0 d   OK

    So `resting_metabolism` was solved for a window, given a fixed
    `max_energy_reserve`. It was never set from a respiration rate, and
    nothing in the repo records one.

118.3 The constraint does not bind, and that is the finding
    Re-running the same tool with only zooplankton's rate changed:

      zoo rm    %/day    T1 (d)   T_total (d)   verdict
      3.5        0.31     16.2       15.0       OK (lit 3-30 d)
      22.5       2.00      2.5        8.2       OK (lit 3-30 d)
      56.2       5.00      1.2        7.5       OK (lit 3-30 d)
      112.5     10.00      0.5        7.2       OK (lit 3-30 d)

    The window stays inside the literature range across a 32-FOLD range
    of `resting_metabolism`, because T_total is dominated by
    `starve_rate = 0.2` catabolising biomass and not by the reserve
    drain. T1 moves by a factor 32; T_total moves by a factor 2.

    That is biologically the right structure - a starving copepod burns
    its lipid store and then its body, and the second phase is the long
    one - but it means the calibration that produced 3.5 had almost no
    power to determine it. Any value in the table passes. The number was
    under-determined and has been treated ever since as though it were
    measured.

118.4 What the literature says
    Sources located (see 118.8): the daily minimum carbon demand of
    NON-FEEDING copepods - respiration plus pellet plus egg production -
    is about 10 % of body weight per day; respiration or excretion
    accounts for roughly 50 % of ingestion in carbon terms; and carbon
    demand for calanoids feeding on dispersed food is 2-3x the resting
    rate.

    Two readings of that against the model:
      * Respiration alone is less than the 10 %/day figure, since that
        figure includes egg production - which in this model IS the
        growth term, not the metabolism. A respiration rate of 2-5 %/day
        is the defensible band, and 0.311 %/day is 1/6 to 1/16 of it.
      * `feeding_cost: 1.45` is the model's feeding multiplier against a
        literature 2-3x. Low, and not investigated here.

118.5 Measured: what the rate does to the world
    `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py --mass-balance --zoo-rm`, 60x60,
    3000 ticks, means over the last 300, frozen greedy, colocated,
    `--mortality on`, library values otherwise. Run under
    `--mass-balance` deliberately: it is the only regime where the tick's
    energy budget closes, so shares of ingested energy mean something
    (the gap column below).

      rm     %/day  resp/ing  built/ing  gap    zoo t   phyto t  zoo:phyto
      3.5     0.31   19.5 %    43.2 %    2.3 %   3959    1448     2.73 : 1
      11.25   1.00   37.6 %    26.0 %    1.4 %   3230    1978     1.63 : 1
      22.5    2.00   46.1 %    17.9 %    1.0 %   2837    2875     0.99 : 1
      56.2    5.00   49.5 %    18.8 %   -3.4 %   5666   22524     0.25 : 1
      112.5  10.00     -          -       -         0   86376     extinct

    (resp/ing and built/ing are shares of INGESTED energy; assimilation
    is 0.65, so the two should come to 65 % and `gap` is what is
    missing. zoo:phyto at spawn is 0.44:1.)

    Reading it:
      * The INVERSION IS A METABOLISM ARTEFACT. zoo:phyto goes 2.73:1 ->
        0.99:1 -> 0.25:1 as the rate rises through its plausible band,
        crossing the spawn ratio 0.44:1 between 2 and 5 %/day. Nothing
        else in sections 109-117 moved it: section 117 showed tripling
        phytoplankton's growth rate made it WORSE.
      * Phytoplankton's standing stock rises with it, 1448 -> 22524 t,
        which is the R* mechanism working in reverse: a more expensive
        grazer breaks even at a higher resource density.
      * There is a WALL just above 5 %/day. At 10 %/day zooplankton goes
        extinct and phytoplankton sits at its carrying capacity. The
        usable band is narrow and its upper edge is close.
      * The respiration share moves onto the literature ~50 % exactly
        where the ratio becomes plausible.

118.6 Two caveats on those numbers
    * The respiration column is an UPPER BOUND. It is computed as
      `biomass * rm * cost_eat`, i.e. as though the whole standing stock
      paid the feeding multiplier every tick; a resting or moving mix
      pays 1.0 or 1.2 instead. The true share is between 69 % and 100 %
      of the printed value - so rm 22.5 sits at 32-46 % of ingestion
      rather than exactly 46 %. The DIRECTION is unaffected: 3.5 gives
      13-19 % against a literature ~50 %.
    * The -3.4 % gap at rm 56.2 is that same over-estimate showing up as
      an impossible closure, which is the check that the bound is a
      bound.

118.7 Correction to sections 114-117: GGE was compared on the wrong basis
    Those sections quote an implied gross growth efficiency of ~19 %
    against a literature copepod GGE of 20-40 % and read it as "inside
    the band, at its floor". The 19 % is a WET MASS ratio, tons of
    zooplankton per ton of phytoplankton; the literature band is CARBON.
    A wet ton of zooplankton carries 4500 MJ against phytoplankton's
    2000, so the carbon-basis figure is 19.2 * 4500/2000 = 43.2 %, i.e.
    ABOVE the band rather than at its floor. That is the `built/ing`
    column in 118.5.

    What this does NOT change: the 28.9 % ceiling of 114.4 is itself a
    mass ratio derived from the same two energy contents, so
    "19.2 % against a 28.9 % ceiling" is apples to apples and stands, as
    does everything built on it - the legacy term's 198-468 % is over the
    ceiling on either basis. What changes is which direction the
    mass-balanced arm errs in: it is slightly too EFFICIENT at rm 3.5,
    not slightly too poor. Raising rm to 22.5 brings it to 17.9 %, just
    under the band.

118.8 Recommendation
    Set `zooplankton.resting_metabolism = 22.5` MJ/t/tick - 2 % of body
    energy per day, a 6.4x raise. It is the value where the respiration
    share reaches the literature order, the zoo:phyto ratio stops being
    inverted (0.99:1), the carbon-basis GGE lands just under the 20-40 %
    band, and the starvation window stays comfortably inside 3-30 days
    at 8.2. 56.2 (5 %/day) is arguably better on the ratio alone but its
    energy accounting does not close, and 112.5 is past the extinction
    wall.

    Do it WITH `--mass-balance`. Under the legacy growth term a higher
    metabolism is partly absorbed by the fabricated biomass and the two
    changes are not separable.

    Not applied - the instruction was to investigate. Companion items
    this raises and does not settle: `max_energy_reserve = 450` (10 % of
    body energy) becomes a 5-day buffer at 2 %/day and a 1-day buffer at
    10 %, so it should be re-derived alongside (115.6 asked for the same
    thing for a different reason); and `feeding_cost: 1.45` against a
    literature 2-3x.

    Sources: Ikeda et al. (2001) Metabolic rates of epipelagic marine
    copepods as a function of body mass and temperature, Mar. Biol.
    139:587-596, for the mass/temperature scaling; and the non-feeding
    carbon-demand, respiration-share and feeding-multiplier figures from
    the copepod metabolic-budget literature located by search rather than
    read in full - they are orders of magnitude, not point citations, and
    should be checked against Ikeda's tables before being quoted as
    settled.

    APPLIED by section 119.

119 APPLIED: ZOOPLANKTON resting_metabolism 3.5 -> 22.5 (2026-09-29)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

    *** ONE TEST IS RED AND UNRESOLVED - READ 119.3 AND 119.7. ***

119.1 The change
    `fgconfig/fg_library.yaml`, zooplankton only:

      resting_metabolism: 3.5  ->  22.5      MJ/t/tick
                                             = 90 MJ/t/day
                                             = 2.0 % of body energy/day

    A 6.4x raise, on the grounds section 118 establishes: 0.311 %/day is
    1/6 to 1/16 of copepod respiration, the starvation window that set
    the old value does not bind (T_total stays inside the literature
    3-30 d across a 32-fold range of this parameter), and 2 %/day is
    where the respiration share reaches the literature order without
    hitting the extinction wall just above 5 %/day.

    No other parameter moved. `growth_rate` stays at 0.0913 (111),
    `natural_mortality` at 0.0025 (108), phytoplankton's `growth_rate`
    at 0.0375 - section 113's recommendation to raise that one was
    still open when this section was written and is APPLIED by 121.

119.2 It presupposes --mass-balance, and that is now a live problem
    Section 118.8 says to make this change with the mass-balanced growth
    term, because under the legacy term a higher metabolism is partly
    absorbed by fabricated biomass. `--mass-balance` is OFF by default
    (116), so as the repo now stands

      python3 train.py --project mareld2.yaml

    runs a zooplankton with 6.4x the metabolism inside a growth term
    that fabricates mass - a combination that is neither calibrated nor
    measured, and worse than either coherent state.

    This is the same shape as 110.5, and it was resolved there by the
    calibration ceasing to presuppose a setting. Here it does not. The
    options are to flip `DEFAULT_MASS_BALANCE` to True, or to revert
    this value. Flipping a default changes every run that does not pass
    the flag and is not a decision to take from a probe result, so it
    was left open.

119.3 Validation: one test turns red, and it is informative
    `tests/test_phyto_zoo_sanity.py::test_zoo_bounded_in_closed_phyto_zoo_system`
    FAILS. Suite 756 passed, 1 failed, 12 skipped (was 757/0/12).

    The test drives a closed phytoplankton + zooplankton world for 200
    ticks (~50 days) from 1000 t each and asserts zooplankton ends
    within a factor 0.03-100 of where it started. At rm 22.5 it ends at
    0.013 of start.

    Attribution, by re-running the same fixture across the rate and both
    growth terms (zoo end / start after 200 ticks):

      rm      legacy   mass-bal   legacy   mass-bal
              default  default    greedy   greedy
      3.5     14.22    1.18       41.31    1.37
      11.25    2.29    0.75        4.04    1.01
      22.5     0.013   0.011       2.52    0.97
      56.2     0.000   0.000       0.23    0.19
      band: PASS is 0.03-100

    The collapse is BEHAVIOURAL, not energetic. The fixture calls
    `env.step()` with no action provider, so the zooplankton makes no
    foraging decisions at all. Give it the viability rig's frozen
    `greedy` behaviour and rm 22.5 passes in both growth terms - and so
    does 56.2. What fails is a zooplankton that does not forage.

    The same table says something about the OLD value. At rm 3.5 with
    the legacy term, zooplankton grows 14x in 50 days WITHOUT FORAGING,
    and 41x with. A metabolism that cheap is why the contract was
    satisfiable; the test was not protecting an ecological property, it
    was measuring how little the metabolism mattered.

    The same fixture defect shows up a second time in a contract that
    still passes. `test_predation_visibly_suppresses_phyto` requires
    zooplankton to depress phytoplankton by more than 0.3 % relative to
    a no-zooplankton baseline:

      rm      phyto alone   with zoo   suppression   margin
      3.5       21279.6     18985.6     10.78 %      x35.9
      11.25     21279.6     20437.9      3.96 %      x13.2
      22.5      21279.6     21174.3      0.50 %       x1.6

    Grazing in the default-action fixture is now barely detectable: one
    more step and that test goes red as well, for the same reason. A
    world where a decision maker does not forage is one where predation
    pressure does not exist, which is what both contracts are really
    measuring.

    Two other test files step decision makers with no action provider -
    `test_population_stability.py` and `test_ecosystem_refactor.py` - so
    whatever is decided here has a scope beyond this one file. Neither
    failed.

    NOT ACTED ON. The fixture, not the threshold, is what looks wrong -
    "the ecosystem must be stable when its decision makers do not
    decide" is a statement about the default-action path, not an
    ecological contract - and the repo-standard fix would be to install
    `greedy` and leave the 0.03-100 band untouched. But editing a test
    so a parameter fits is the inversion this whole arc has been
    correcting, so it is a decision to take deliberately and not a
    side effect of a calibration. The suite is left red.

119.4 Validation: everything else
    * The library parses; only zooplankton's rate moved.
    * One-generation training run with `--mortality on --mass-balance`
      completed.
    * `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py` at the LIBRARY value
      reproduces what 118.5 predicted from an in-memory override:

                        predicted (118.5)   measured (119)
        respired/ing       46.1 %             46.6 %
        built/ing          17.9 %             17.5 %
        closure gap         1.0 %              0.9 %
        zoo                2837 t             2835 t
        phyto              2875 t             2845 t
        zoo:phyto          0.99 : 1           1.00 : 1

      The inversion is gone: 2.73:1 at rm 3.5, 1.00:1 now, against a
      spawn ratio of 0.44:1.

    * The same probe WITHOUT `--mass-balance` is the quantified proof of
      119.2: respired 67.9 % and built 102.5 % of ingested energy, a
      closure gap of -105 %, and a mass-basis efficiency of 45.6 %
      against the 28.9 % thermodynamic ceiling. The legacy term does not
      merely absorb the higher metabolism, it fabricates harder to pay
      for it. This value must not be used without the flag.

119.5 The viability corner got worse by one group
    `--spawn colocated --behaviour greedy --mortality on`, 20x20, 7300
    ticks, 3 seeds, both at rm 22.5:

      fg             mass-balance        legacy
      gadoids        FAIL below floor    FAIL below floor
      pelagic_fish   FAIL below floor    FAIL below floor
      porpoises      FAIL extinct@62     FAIL extinct@106
      benthic        PASS 0.832          PASS 0.706
      phytoplankton  FAIL 0.034          FAIL 0.093
      zooplankton    FAIL 0.080          PASS 0.254
      overall        NOT VIABLE          NOT VIABLE

    Against rm 3.5 with the flag (116.5): phytoplankton improves,
    0.018 -> 0.034, and zooplankton drops from 0.113 to 0.080, i.e. from
    just above the criterion's 0.1 floor to just below it. Five failing
    groups where there were four.

    That is not a grid artefact - the 60x60 probe puts zooplankton at
    2835 t against an inference spawn of 35000, which is the same 0.08.

119.6 The level is wrong, the ratio is not
    Both plankton groups now sit at 3-8 % of their `mareld2.yaml` spawn
    levels while holding a zoo:phyto ratio of 1.00:1 against a spawn
    ratio of 0.44:1. Section 113.9 argued those spawn levels look
    biologically anchored - phytoplankton at 93 % of its carrying
    capacity, zooplankton at 0.44 of it. So the model now gets the
    RELATIVE structure of the plankton right and the ABSOLUTE level
    wrong by a factor 15-30.

    The obvious next combination is this value together with section
    113's phytoplankton 0.125, which 117 showed raises throughput 3.3x
    and zooplankton 3.2x while barely moving phytoplankton's own stock.
    That is the arm most likely to fix the level without breaking the
    ratio. Not run.

119.7 Standing recommendation
    Either flip `DEFAULT_MASS_BALANCE` to True or revert this value;
    119.2 and 119.4 together say the pair is not optional. And the red
    test in 119.3 needs a decision of its own before the suite is green
    again. If the preference is to keep the suite green today, rm 11.25
    (1 %/day) passes every arm of 119.3 and still triples the
    metabolism, at the cost of a zoo:phyto of 1.63:1 instead of 1.00:1
    and a respiration share of 37.6 % instead of 46.6 %.

120 FIXTURE FIXED, --mass-balance ON BY DEFAULT, AND THE COMBINATION MEASURED (2026-09-29)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    The three decisions 119.7 left open, all taken.

120.1 The sanity fixture now forages
    `tests/test_phyto_zoo_sanity.py::_build_env` called `env.step()` with
    no action provider, so its zooplankton never made a foraging
    decision, and all four contracts in the file were measuring an
    ecosystem whose decision makers do not decide. It now installs the
    viability rig's frozen `greedy`, which is the repo's standard for
    behaviour-free measurement, and skips it for the phyto-only baseline
    that has no decision makers.

    THE BANDS ARE UNCHANGED. Only the behaviour is. That is the whole of
    the change: no threshold was widened to fit a parameter.

    Why the fixture and not the threshold, stated so the argument can be
    checked rather than trusted: at the OLD `resting_metabolism` of 3.5
    the non-foraging zooplankton grew 14x in 50 days, and 41x with
    greedy. The green state was the absurd one. The grazing-pressure
    contract in the same file said it from the other side - its margin
    against the 0.3 % threshold fell 36x -> 13x -> 1.6x as the
    metabolism went 3.5 -> 11.25 -> 22.5, because a grazer that does not
    forage exerts no grazing pressure, so that test was about to go red
    for the same reason.

    `test_population_stability.py` and `test_ecosystem_refactor.py` also
    step decision makers with no action provider. Neither failed and
    neither was touched; the same question applies to them.

120.2 --mass-balance is ON by default
    `DEFAULT_MASS_BALANCE = True`, and `EcosystemEnvironment`'s
    constructor default with it. The library is calibrated for that tick
    - zooplankton's `growth_rate` (111) and `resting_metabolism` (118)
    are both set in that world - so a run that passes no flag has to get
    it. 119.4 is the quantified version: with the legacy term at the
    current library, zooplankton converts mass at 45.6 % against a
    28.9 % thermodynamic ceiling and the energy budget over-accounts by
    105 %.

    `--mass-balance` is kept as a no-op so scripts written against
    section 116 keep working, and `--no-mass-balance` is the way back to
    the pre-116 tick. Its help text says the library is not calibrated
    for it.

    One thing that must NOT follow the default: what an OLD run was
    doing. `LEGACY_MASS_BALANCE = False` is a separate constant and
    `training_progress.comparable_config` defaults histories to it, so
    flipping the default cannot retroactively relabel a recorded run.
    Asserted by `test_legacy_constant_is_separate_from_the_default`.

    Caught while doing this, and worth recording because it is the same
    trap one level down: `tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py` and
    `zoo_growth_sweep.py` each had their own `--mass-balance`
    `store_true` with an implicit default of False. After the flip they
    silently forced the LEGACY term - the first run of 120.3 came back
    with a -139 % closure gap before that was found. Both now default to
    `DEFAULT_MASS_BALANCE` and carry `--no-mass-balance`.

    Suite: 760 passed, 12 skipped, 0 failed. Was 756/1/12 at the end of
    section 119.

120.3 The combination: phytoplankton 0.125 with resting_metabolism 22.5
    Run here with the rate overridden in memory; APPLIED to the library
    by section 121, which reproduces the numbers below to within a
    percent. 60x60,
    3000 ticks, frozen greedy, colocated, `--mortality on`, means over
    the last 300 ticks:

                          phyto 0.0375      phyto 0.125
      zoo                   2835 t            9578 t
        vs spawn             0.081x            0.274x
      phyto                 2845 t            4080 t
        vs spawn             0.036x            0.051x
      zoo:phyto             1.00 : 1          2.35 : 1
      phyto grazed          99.3 t/tick      387.1 t/tick
      respired / ingested   46.6 %            40.4 %
      built / ingested      17.5 %            25.0 %
      closure gap            0.9 %            -0.3 %

    The `built / ingested` row is the result. 25.0 % is INSIDE the
    literature copepod gross growth efficiency band of 20-40 % on a
    carbon basis - the first time any arm in sections 108-120 has landed
    there. The respiration share stays at the literature order and the
    energy budget still closes.

    The level moves the way 119.6 predicted: zooplankton 3.4x and
    phytoplankton 1.4x, so the plankton sit at 5-27 % of their spawn
    levels instead of 4-8 %. Still low, but the gap is a factor 4-20
    where it was 15-30.

    The ratio moves the WRONG way, 1.00:1 -> 2.35:1, which is exactly
    section 117.2's R* invariance doing its work: a faster resource
    raises throughput and the consumer's stock but not its own, so the
    ratio degrades. Raising phytoplankton buys level at the cost of
    ratio; raising the metabolism buys ratio at the cost of level. They
    are not the same lever and neither fixes both.

    Food web, 3 seeds, final-window means over spawn:

      arm                          phy_end  zoo_end  zoo_min  gad_end
      rm 3.5,  phyto 0.125 (117)    0.020    0.362    0.361   0.37-0.51
      rm 22.5, phyto 0.125          0.046    0.268    0.142   0.46-0.58
      (3-seed spread on the last row: 0.046-0.050, 0.268-0.271,
       0.142-0.169)

    Gadoids improve and phytoplankton more than doubles.

    Viability corner, `--mortality on`, 20x20, 7300 ticks, 3 seeds:

      arm                        zooplankton      phytoplankton   FAILs
      rm 3.5,  phyto 0.0375      PASS 0.113       FAIL 0.018        4
      rm 22.5, phyto 0.0375      FAIL 0.080       FAIL 0.034        5
      rm 22.5, phyto 0.125       PASS 0.256       FAIL 0.041        4

    The combination recovers the criterion that 119.5 lost: zooplankton
    goes from 0.080, just under the 0.1 floor, back to 0.256. Overall
    still NOT VIABLE on the same three pre-existing failures.

120.4 Where this leaves the open list
    Applied: mass-balanced growth (116, now default), zooplankton
    `growth_rate` 0.0913 (111), `natural_mortality` M1 values (108),
    `resting_metabolism` 22.5 (119).

    Open, in the order the evidence now supports:
      1. CLOSED by 121: phytoplankton `growth_rate` 0.0375 -> 0.125
         (113), applied. It is the only lever that moves the absolute
         level, and its cost is the zoo:phyto ratio.
      2. CLOSED by 124 (-> 675). `max_energy_reserve` 450 MJ/t for zooplankton. At 2 %/day it is
         a 5-day buffer where it used to be 32, and under `--mass-balance`
         it is also the growth budget - `max_energy_reserve /
         energy_content = 0.100` is a hard ceiling on the reachable
         `growth_rate`, and 0.0913 sits at 91 % of it (115.3, 116.2).
      3. CLOSED by 124 (-> 2.0). `feeding_cost` 1.45 against a literature 2-3x (118.4).
      4. The absolute plankton level, still a factor 4-20 below spawn
         even in the best arm. SCOPE: measured under FROZEN GREEDY
         behaviour only (the viability corner, no trained policy). A
         trained co-evolved run holds phytoplankton at 0.56x and
         zooplankton at 2.3x spawn, stationary over 10000 ticks -
         section 123. Not an open item for trained runs.
      5. The no-action fixtures in `test_population_stability.py` and
         `test_ecosystem_refactor.py` (120.1).

122 Strategi_sep.pdf: THE STRATEGY DECK REWRITTEN AGAINST THE CODE (2026-09-29)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

122.1 What was made
    `Strategi_sep.pdf` - a 60-slide, 960x540 pt Swedish deck that
    replaces the May 2026 `Strategi.pdf` (37 slides, PowerPoint export).
    Same section order; the content is written against the tick, the
    trainer and the library as they stand after section 121.

    Generator: `tools/make_strategi_deck.py` (matplotlib PdfPages, no
    LaTeX or pandoc in this environment). Re-run it when the mechanics
    move:

        python3 tools/make_strategi_deck.py -o Strategi_sep.pdf
        python3 tools/make_strategi_deck.py --png <dir>   # proofing

    The spawn-strategy slide calls `lib/spawn/strategies.make_weights`
    directly, so those four panels are the real fields, not mock-ups.

122.2 Where the deck deviates from the May edition, and why
    One slide states these explicitly, because the May document is a
    draft to develop further and not a spec to conform to:

      growth term      mass-balanced and charged to the reserve (114-116,
                       120); the documented B*(1+MG*q) was free mass
      hunger           the May deck had BOTH the s > 0.8 eat mask and
                       h = max(0, 1 - s/0.8). The code kept the factor,
                       now per-FG via satiation_scale (69), and dropped
                       the mask. Remaining difference: mass that eats
                       while satiated still pays feeding_cost
      predation        Holling II/III + Beddington-DeAngelis (86), the
                       0.999 harvest cap (73), per-pair visibility
                       floors (71.12)
      fitness          mean_t log(E_tot(t)/E_tot(0)) with
                       E_tot = B*energy_content + R; alpha/beta survives
                       as --legacyreward only
      rollouts         15-200 ticks, not the documented H = 2
      training         co-evolution (all DMs perturbed in one shared
                       rollout) instead of the alternating schedule
      memory           the N(c,t-1) extension is not implemented; the
                       only cross-tick state is prev_hidden_frac
      size ladder      3x3 -> 60x60 is not the recommended flow; --grid
                       remains and the viability rig runs 20x20

    New sections with no counterpart in the May edition: tick length as
    a run parameter (97, 103-106), currents, migration, the extinction
    threshold and split suppression (69), the local per-cell reward
    (79-84), the viability rig (90-92, VIABILITY.md), the energy budget
    gate, and the status table from 121.3.

122.3 Checks
    Text is Swedish (the original deck's language); parameter names stay
    in their English library spelling. Word wrapping is measured against
    the real glyph advances, and a checker asserts that no text artist
    leaves the page or drops into the footer band - 0 problems over all
    60 slides. MediaBox verified as [0 0 960 540], matching the original
    export. Nothing in the engine, the library or the tests was touched.

123 PLANKTON LEVEL UNDER TRAINED POLICIES: THE 4-20x GAP IS A FROZEN-
    BEHAVIOUR ARTEFACT (2026-09-29)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

123.1 Why
    120.3/120.4/121.4 reported the absolute plankton level as "a factor
    4-20 below spawn". That number comes from the frozen greedy corner
    (every DM eats maximally, no policy), 60x60, 3000 ticks, no
    migration or currents. It was then repeated as if it held for the
    model in general. It does not.

123.2 Measurement
    Run `pzbpgp248nsrpfix35` (train_gpu.py, co-evolution, --profile
    info, policynetwork 2 48 tanh, 500-tick rollouts, ~295 iterations
    over 6 generations, --mortality on --migration on --currents on,
    tick length 6 h, library as of section 121). Inference, headless:

        python3 inference.py --project mareld2.yaml \
            --run-name pzbpgp248nsrpfix35 --ticks 10000 --seed 1 \
            --mortality on --migration on --currents on --tick-length 6 \
            --quiet --output inf10k.npz

    Biomass / spawn, window means (10000 ticks = ~6.8 years, 2 min):

      fg             t~500  t~2000  t~5000  t~7500  t9500-10000  drift*
      phytoplankton  0.56   0.55    0.56    0.56    0.56         1.00
      zooplankton    2.31   2.19    2.21    2.40    2.29         1.01
      benthic        0.98   0.86    0.72    0.49    0.65         0.96
      gadoids        1.15   1.74    3.13    1.29    1.09         0.44
      pelagic_fish   1.18   1.74    1.71    0.97    1.38         0.94
      porpoises      0.15   0.008   0       0       0            -

      * mean(7500-10000) / mean(5000-7500)

    The training log agrees: at tick 500 of every rollout from gen 1
    iter 21 onwards, phytoplankton reads 0.55-0.58x and zooplankton
    1.9-2.3x spawn.

123.3 Reading
    - Both plankton groups are stationary from tick ~2000 to 10000
      (within +-5 %). phyto ~44 kt, zoo ~80 kt. The absolute level is
      healthy under trained policies; item 4 of 120.4 is closed for
      trained runs and stays a property of the frozen corner only.
    - What remains is the ratio: zoo:phyto 1.80:1 against a spawn
      0.44:1 (the frozen corner gives 2.34:1). That is the R*
      structure of 117.2, not a level deficit.
    - Porpoises go extinct under the trained policy as well: below
      10 % of spawn at tick 738, zero at tick 2365 (~1.6 years). Same
      failure as the viability corner (121.3).
    - Gadoids and pelagic_fish run a slow cycle (gadoids peak 3.1x at
      t~5000, back to 1.0x at t~9000) with benthic dipping to 0.5x.
      Period is of order the run length; 10000 ticks and one seed
      cannot say whether it damps.

123.4 Caveat
    One seed, one checkpoint set. Nothing in engine, library or tests
    was changed. The same overbroad claim sat on slide 59 ("Oppna
    fragor") of `Strategi_sep.pdf` (section 122); the bullet in
    `tools/make_strategi_deck.py` now gives the trained-run levels and
    scopes the 4-20x figure to frozen greedy behaviour, and the PDF was
    regenerated.

124 APPLIED: ZOOPLANKTON feeding_cost 1.45 -> 2.0, max_energy_reserve
    450 -> 675 (2026-09-29)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

124.1 What was open
    The two zooplankton parameters 118.8, 120.4 and 121.4 left as not
    set from the literature: `feeding_cost` 1.45 (against a quoted
    "2-3x") and `max_energy_reserve` 450 MJ/t (10 % of body energy,
    fitted to a starvation window, and under --mass-balance also the
    growth ceiling ME/ec = 0.100 with growth_rate at 91 % of it,
    115.3/115.6). Plus 118.8's caveat that the copepod figures had been
    located by search and not read.

    Assemblage (decided by the user): a MIXED Skagerrak mesozooplankton,
    small neritic copepods (Acartia, Temora, Pseudocalanus, Centropages,
    Oithona) with a Calanus finmarchicus/helgolandicus component,
    weighted 2/3 : 1/3 by biomass. 8-12 C.

124.2 Literature
    Tags: [V] number read in the source (abstract or text), [2H] quoted
    second-hand, [C] computed (lipid 39.5 kJ/g, non-lipid 23.6 kJ/g).

    Feeding cost = respiration fed / respiration starved:
      Acartia tonsa        1.9          Thor 2000, JEMBE 245:171 [V/2H]
      A. clausi, C. helgo. 1.6-2.5      Svetlichny et al. 2022 [V]
      Temora/Acartia/Cent. ~2           Koski et al. 2017, PLoS ONE [V]
      -> band 1.8-2.5, central 2.0. The "2-3x" is Koski's TOTAL carbon
      demand of feeding calanoids (respiration + pellets + eggs), not a
      respiration ratio. Eggs are this model's growth term, so the
      respiration ratio is the one that belongs in feeding_cost.

    Storage lipid as a share of body energy:
      small neritic copepods   total lipid 3-7 % of DM (Kreibich 2008
                               [V], Svetlichny 2022 [V]), mostly
                               phospholipid -> mobilisable 3-6 % [C]
      Calanus CV active season 20-40 % lipid of DM (inferred; wax ester
                               ~90 % of lipid, Kattner & Krause 1987 [V])
                               -> 25-45 % of body energy [C]
      -> assemblage ~15 % (band 10-25 %).
    Starvation: lipid exhausted after ~3-7 d, mortality rises after
    ~1-2 weeks for the assemblage (Dagg 1977 [2H], Tsuda 1994 [2H],
    Kreibich 2008 [V], Niehoff 2000 [V]). Copepods respire 50-65 % of
    body carbon before death - most of it protein after the lipid is
    gone, which is the model's starve_rate phase, not the reserve.

    Resting rate (cross-check of 119, not changed): starved Acartia
    ~2.4 %/day at 10 C [C from Svetlichny]; in-situ Temora 3.9-6.0
    %/day (Castellani & Altunbas 2014 [V]); Ikeda et al. 2001 (fasting,
    units inferred, not read) predicts ~7.5 %/day for a 5 ug C copepod
    and ~3.2 %/day for a Calanus CV. So 22.5 MJ/t/tick = 2.0 %/day reads
    as the FASTING floor, and with feeding_cost 2.0 the feeding rate is
    4 %/day, inside the measured in-situ band. The two parameters are
    consistent without double-charging. 2.0 %/day stays at the low edge
    of the literature and is the one number here Ikeda would raise.

    Energy density: 4.1-5.4 kJ/g wet for the mix [C from Laurence 1976,
    McKinstry 2013, via 2H]; 4500 MJ/t unchanged.

    Not read in full (paywall): Lee, Hagen & Kattner 2006; Kiorboe et
    al. 1985; Ikeda et al. 2001; Tsuda 1994; Dagg 1977.

124.3 Checks
    Energy-balance gate: max allowed feeding_cost at rm 22.5 is
    h(u) * ceiling / rm = 0.375 * 325 / 22.5 = 5.42; 2.0 passes.

    tools/starve_calibration.py (PYTHONPATH=. needed when run directly):
      ME 450   T1 2.5 d   T_total 8.2 d
      ME 675   T1 3.8 d   T_total 8.8 d   (lit: lipid 3-7 d, death 1-2 w)

    Growth ceiling under --mass-balance: ME/ec 0.100 -> 0.150, so
    growth_rate 0.0913 is no longer at 91 % of the bound.

124.4 Measured
    tools/probes/growth_mass_balance.py, extended in this section with
    --zoo-fc, --zoo-me (the spawned reserve is rescaled so s_X(0) is
    unchanged), --behaviour policy --checkpoints DIR (configured spawn,
    trained policies), --migration and --currents. Default behaviour is
    unchanged.

    Run-to-run spread, found and FIXED in this section. The numbers below
    were measured before the fix, when repeated default runs with the
    same --seed gave zoo 9326-9589 t and phyto 3705-4077 t, about +-3 %.
    Cause: benthic_community has seed_rate 1e-06, and its recruitment
    noise (population_change.py, np.random.uniform) is drawn from NumPy's
    GLOBAL RNG, which inference.build_env never seeded - --seed reached
    the spawn only. Two runs differed from tick 0 (benthic, 6e-9
    relative) and the predator-prey dynamics amplified that to per cent
    over 3000 ticks. Isolated by elimination: fixed PYTHONHASHSEED and
    OMP/MKL_NUM_THREADS=1 still diverged; np.random.seed(0) gave
    bit-identical 3000-tick traces; benthic seed_rate 0 with the global
    RNG unseeded also gave bit-identical traces. The per-tick FG shuffle
    (environment.py, np.random.shuffle) uses the same RNG but does not
    change the result - every FG's update is independent of the order.

    Fix: build_env(seed=...) now also seeds the global RNG
    (np.random.seed(seed & 0xFFFFFFFF)); seed=None leaves it alone.
    Covers inference.py, tools/viability.py, api.py and every probe
    built on build_env. Training is untouched: train.py workers seed the
    global RNG per rollout (parallel_worker) and the GPU engine seeds in
    lib/gpu/config.py. After the fix two full growth_mass_balance.py
    runs print identical output. Regression test:
    tests/test_inference_determinism.py (fails without the fix).

    Consequences: differences under ~3 % between two single rollouts
    measured before this fix are noise - in the tables below that is the
    reserve's effect on the levels under trained policies (-4 %). The
    3 % also says something about the model: a 1e-5 t/cell/tick
    perturbation grows to per cent in 3000 ticks, so single-seed
    comparisons should use several seeds, not one.

    Trained policies (results/pzbpgp248nsrpfix35, migration + currents
    on, 3000 ticks, last 300):
      fc     ME     zoo t    phyto t   zoo:phyto   GGE wet   GGE carbon
      1.45   450    75322    44567     1.69:1      11.8 %    26.6 %
      2.0    450    65290    44364     1.47:1      10.8 %    24.3 %
      2.5    450    56493    44029     1.28:1       9.7 %    21.8 %
      2.0    675    62525    45454     1.38:1      10.6 %    23.9 %
    (carbon = wet * 4500/2000, 118.7)

    Frozen greedy, colocated (mechanics bench, not a verdict - 123):
      fc     ME     zoo t    phyto t   zoo:phyto   built/ingested
      1.45   450     9475     3945     2.40:1      23.9 %
      2.0    450    10717     7437     1.44:1      24.7 %
      2.5    450    14228    16806     0.85:1      25.3 %
      3.0    450    16645    26075     0.64:1      22.6 %
      2.0    675    14669    13597     1.08:1      26.8 %

    Reading: under trained policies phytoplankton does not move and
    zooplankton drops 17 %; almost all of it is feeding_cost, the
    reserve is within noise on the levels. Under frozen greedy a higher
    feeding_cost raises R* - the classic result 115.7 predicted for the
    maintenance/intake triple - and both levels rise. Carbon-basis GGE
    stays inside the copepod 20-40 % band in every arm.

124.5 Applied
    fgconfig/fg_library.yaml, zooplankton:
      feeding_cost        1.45  -> 2.0
      max_energy_reserve  450.0 -> 675.0
    with the derivation in the comments; the resting_metabolism comment
    now says it is the fasting rate. Docstring in
    population_change._charge_growth_to_reserve updated (0.150). The
    strategy deck (122) was updated to the new values and regenerated.

    Validation: suite 760 passed, 12 skipped (761 with the determinism
    test of 124.4). 1-generation train.py run
    with --mortality on completed; gen-1 probe: phytoplankton 98 %,
    zooplankton 86 %, porpoises 36 % of spawn.

    Checkpoints: no observation shape moved, but the reserve change
    shifts the fill level s_X that the zooplankton policy observes, so
    policies trained before this section see a shifted input and should
    be retrained before being quoted.

124.6 Still open for plankton
    - resting_metabolism 2.0 %/day at the low edge (Ikeda would raise it;
      needs Ikeda's tables read, not inferred).
    - the no-action fixtures in test_population_stability.py and
      test_ecosystem_refactor.py (120.1).
